Named in the unit table now (`shape: tank`, sheets PIR_TANK and PIR_TANK_L): a hull box, a turret box on its deck and a hollow barrel, their sizes found by optimising how well their outlines match its eight drawings; then each box cut by what the drawings leave (the hull's ends and the turret's sides slope as drawn), the belly hollow between the tracks as the front and back drawings show it, and the tank as high as the game's tanks stand (its deck at a kneeling soldier's height, its turret's top at 20 voxels, the owner's rule for tanks), painted from the drawings as they are drawn: the game gives the colour group of a soldier's hair the colour of her hair, and the tank, worn by a gal, has its treads' darkest shade in that group, so its treads came out in her green (the owner: "The tank side view of tracks should not have green pixels/voxels!"). Every other unit's body is unchanged (the zoo save's 18 others, byte for byte).
- *Voxel units*: **XPiratez's government men have their backs whole** (a player, GitHub issue #264: "my crosshair SHOULD be on backpack, but its like a chunk was removed"). The Enforcer, the Elite and the Rogue Colonel and Officer wear one helmet over narrower shoulders, which the bodies took for a big head: the back under it was cut to a thin neck, a notch between the backpack and the helmet. The unit table's `neck: none` names their sheets now (GOV_2, GOV_3, GOV_10, GOV_11); in the tester's save their seven bodies have their backs filled in, and the 20 other bodies there (the agents, the pilots, the general, a tank, the squad) are byte for byte as they were.
- *Reflections* on **the Steam Deck (Mesa's AMD driver, radeonsi): a paddy's mirror no longer goes out** a second or two after the 3D view opens (a player, GitHub issue #261, who confirmed that drawing the mirrors in one go keeps it). On that driver the mirrors are now drawn in one go, as they were before 1.15 (every pixel through the mirrors' program: a frame with mirrors costs more there); every other card keeps the two goes.
- **Obliteration** (a player, GitHub issue #257: "it would be SICK to gib a ratman with a sniper"; the owner: "Lets just do obliteration effect - also bodies on the floor"): a new setting under Graphics, YES by default. A unit killed beyond the game's own overkill (`BattleUnit::getOverKillDamage`, its rule for a unit that leaves no corpse) bursts in 3D into the pieces of its body at the hit, and a blast that destroys a body lying on the floor bursts it too. The pieces are the body's own cubes (*Voxel units*) or its picture's pixels (its dark outline left out), fly away from the side struck, char as they fly as the map burns such a unit over its fall, land, bounce off walls and lie a few seconds; flesh bleeds, machines spark. The engine tells the 3D view at the hit (TileEngine::hitUnit) and before a blast removes a body (TileEngine::explode); the pieces are particles of a new kind that meet the world's voxels. No stains on walls or floors for now.
- **Quitting soon after a start no longer crashes** (*Renderer* GPU, the first start after an update or with the driver's shader cache empty). The game was quit while the graphics card's driver was still building the card's programs (tens of seconds the first time), and the driver's own clean-up at the program's end ran under the building: a crash at exit, in the driver. Now the game's end stops the building after the program being built, closes the renderer and only then quits.
- *Metallic surfaces*: **the flashlight's gleam on a hull is half as strong**. Close under it at night (about 7 tiles) a hull's drawing went nearly white; at 11 tiles it read well.
- *Reflections*: **window glass mirrors without *Smooth light* too** (it did only with it), as the sea, still water and metal did already.
- *See-through corpses* is **off by default and in every Gameplay preset** (it was on from Extended+). A profile on a named preset is moved over once (the tables' third change); a mix of your own keeps it as it is.
- Settings: **Voxel units left the first page**: it is under Advanced only, the very last setting, in a new section EXPERIMENTAL; **CHEATS** come just before it, after all the others (they stood among them by the alphabet).
- *Voxel units* in XPiratez (a player, GitHub issue #259, by name in the unit table as ever): **the Academy's students and nurses** (sheets SCI_12 and SCI_11) are standard humanoids with the gals' depth and waist (the owner: "Both"), no longer a block of chest and belly; **the men drawn on the soldiers' sheets** (the 38 armours on the male body: the slaves', a hero's karate outfit...) have their waist filled out again (the gals' waist as drawn had drawn theirs in to a thread); **the war dogs' ears** stand apart (new word `ears: apart`: the tips of ears drawn higher side on than from the front had stood as one column over the middle of the head, a hat). And **a craft's nose no longer shows pink** from its blunt end: the art paints that hidden end a pink no one sees on the map (palette colour 195), and a new table, `filler:`, names such sets; their filler takes the nearest of the part's own colours.
- **Damage numbers: Overkill** (a player, GitHub issue #262; the owner: "as an expanded setting of damage numbers"): the row is now **Off**, **On** or **Overkill**. With Overkill a killing hit shows, in orange after the red, the health it took past what the unit had (most damage types stop health at 0, so the red number alone showed only what was left). The engine notes the hit's health damage before it floors it (BattleUnit::damage).
- *Voxel units*: **XPiratez's Clickor has a waist again** (a player, GitHub issue #259: "just spinal cord between chest and hips"), and so do The X-Com Files' antmen, drawn the same. Their arms' frames are the lower claws, which begin at the waist, so the bodies took the whole chest for a big head and the waist for its chin and neck: a shallow piece at the front, a thin column behind, the claws hanging free beside it. A new word in the unit table, `neck: none`, names them: the trunk is as drawn down to the hips. Every other body is as it was.
- **A paddy's rice is green** (a player, GitHub issue #261: "all of its actual sprite data is green"). On still water (the mod's `stillWater:` table) what stands above the water's face, a paddy's rice (the game's own voxels, thin stalks) and the mud and moss islands of the paddies and swamps, took the colour of the water the art draws behind it and stood blue. It takes the art's nearest colour that isn't water now. And the green the rice's tufts painted on the water round the stalks (the art's tufts laid flat) is water now: the green is the stalks' alone ("green paint should be limited TO the stock"), on a floor where all that stands above the water is thin stalks; the islands keep theirs. Everything else is as it was.
- **No breath bubbles on land** (a player, GitHub issue #260): with *Voxel units*, aquanauts, aliens and tanks breathed bubbles on TFTD's land missions too. They breathe as the map draws it now: under the sea only, in armour that has bubbles, with no floor overhead.
- **Fewer settings** (the owner went over every one: "Implement all that you suggest ... keep all the hidden options in code easily accessible"). **Gone from the tab, always as their default:** *Each pellet on its own*, *Shotgun pellets in flight*, *Smooth fire*, *Melee hits floors* (with *Melee hits walls and downed*), *Camera when a soldier falls* (with *Aliens' turn cameras*), *Facing icons*, *Middle click identifies*, *Craft icons*, *Check the landing*, *Lift beams* (with *Particles*), *Eye height lowered by* (3), *Ghost opacity* (50%), *Tactical map: wheel changes level*, *Hit flash* (with *Muzzle flashes*), *Force arcing shots* and *Right click opens doors*. **One row for several:** *Tactical map* Off / In 3D / Both views (with the isometric view's), *Shot info* Off / Basic / Costs / Damage / Full (with *Expanded crosshair info* and *Shot damage*), *Alien turn* Map / 3D / Facing (with *look as the soldier faces*), *Shadows* Off / On, and on the card Soft / Lamps / Soft+lamps (with *Soft sun shadows* and *Shadows from every light*), *See-through* Off / Crops / All (crops and corpses), *Flight cost per level* Rules / 0 to 24 (with *Flight cost overrides rules*). The game's own *Frame rate limit* and frame counter are left to its General tab and F7; the VR settings to the Meta Quest build; on the desktop *Sprite upscaling* is Finest or Off (Smooth and Smooth+ become Finest). *Smooth light* is now **Smooth colours** (it was too like *Smooth lighting*). Every one keeps its code and its line in the profile: see "Fixed, off the tab" in section 4; OPENXCOM3D_ALL_SETTINGS=1 lists them all again. Advanced lists 101 settings on the desktop, where it listed 135.
- **OK on the options screen keeps the window.** The game's options reset the display on every OK, whatever was changed, and the SDL layer the Windows and macOS downloads use made a new window each time (on Windows in fullscreen, on macOS always): the game seemed to close and open again, and a stream capturing its window lost it (a player's report, on switching *Voxel units*). With our mod on, a reset that would make the same video mode (size, colours, flags) now keeps the one there is; a change of the screen's settings still makes a new one.
- The manual's Gameplay table is the code's again (the #193 rulings: XMbl leans with a tilt, jumps and takes fall damage; the downed always marked from Extended+ on; every room's walls painted and guns first in all six; smooth lighting from XMbl on).

### 1.15 (2026-11-22)

Hot-fixed 2026-11-23 (the first 21 entries, folded into the release in two goes the same day):

- **Detailed hit log** (new setting, off; the owner: "We want to keep the og hit log in 3D window exactly. (Maybe enhance it if we can)", then on the choices: inside the log window, with who was hit, the damage numbers, and kills and knock-outs). Compared first with OpenXcom Extended without the add-on, the same saves and the same dice: the hit log's window and the diary read the same on the map and in 3D for single shots, an auto burst, a rocket into a group (one word for each unit the blast hurt), reaction fire and blows; the log names only your weapon, never a unit, and shows no numbers; the diary's white and orange lines only alternate. Off, nothing of it changes (the same calls as ever). On: the game's hit (`TileEngine::hitUnit`) asks `FirstPerson::hitLogDetail` for a text, "(Sectoid Soldier 30, stun 2, killed) ", from the health and stun the hit took, the unit's name as the game gives it and whether it was out before the hit (`isOutThresholdExceed`), and the log puts it after the hit's word (`HitLog::appendToHitLog` with a text, for the three hit words; everything else about the log as it was). A unit the squad doesn't see (not ours, not visible) is "unseen", with no numbers, unless in the debug mode. Seen in both views: the burst, the rocket into the group (three named, one unseen), reaction fire (our soldier named by his name), two stun blows ("down"); and with the setting off, exactly the original
- *Metallic surfaces*: **one switch, Off or On, and the metal speckled** (the owner: "for the metal surfaces have only mirror and sparkle setting combined as metal surfaces on/off. Off is off - on is mirror metal. Finish with speckle also"). It was Off, Sheen (the glints) and Mirror (the glints and the mirror, only with *Reflections* on). On is now both. The mirror first needed *Reflections* no more (that setting made the glass's and the water's alone); the same night the owner asked whether *Reflections* off should turn every reflection off, the metal's too, and ruled "Yes let the reflections turn off all reflections!": *Reflections* is the switch of every mirror, glass, water and metal (`frame.reflections`, uniform `uReflections`, as before), and *Metallic surfaces* On without it glints and sparkles only. The option keeps its id and stays a number, 0 or more (a profile with Sheen, 1, or Mirror, 2, reads On; made a yes/no it would have failed on an old profile's 2 and the game would have thrown the whole profile away, as `fpsFlashlight` once did). "Speckle", as read here: the metal has flakes, one voxel in `MetalFlakeEvery` (5), each tilted up to `MetalFlakeTilt` (0.35) its own way by a hash of its voxel, glinting `MetalFlake` (3) shades as tight as `MetalFlakeShine` (40) where the sun or a lamp catches it, so the hull sparkles as you move. Seen on the UFO by day, Off and On, and On with *Reflections* off (glints and flakes, no mirror)
- *Reflections*: **the units in the mirrors, yours and the enemy's, and yourself** (the owner: "Windows and metals should also have unit and enemy reflections besides the world"; it had been left out on his word in issue #224, "glass mirrors the surroundings only"). Window glass, still water, the sea and a UFO's metal (*Metallic surfaces* Mirror) now show who stands before them: every standing unit the squad sees within twenty tiles of the eye, the nearest twelve, the soldier looked through too, who sees himself in a pane. How: the mirrored ray, after it has met the world, is tested against each unit's upright card, the same card with the same picture that the lines of fire test with *Realistic hitboxes* (`FirstPerson::mirrorCard`, `spriteColorsOf` beside `spriteOf`; `mirrorUnit` in the ray shader, called from the glass mirror and from the water and metal mirror): the card is turned square to where the ray comes from, across the map, and carries the unit's own drawing of the side seen from there, of its eight, so a mirror shows the side of a unit that is turned to it; lit as the unit is where it stands. The drawings also with *Voxel units* on: the cube bodies are drawn apart from the rays and no ray can walk them; the bodies are made from those drawings. An enemy the squad does not see is in no mirror. The numbers go to the card behind the crafts' mirrors, in the same buffer (`FirstPersonView::mirrorUnits`, `sendMirrors`, `uMirrorUnits`): the count, eight numbers a unit, its eight pictures of 64 by 64, four pixels a number; a unit's pictures are kept while it holds its pose and made again every 400 ms. Cost, measured on the card at 1920x1080 with a dozen units near a UFO: a quarter of a millisecond a frame (12.3 to 12.8 ms); nothing with *Reflections* off; not in a headset's eyes. Seen: a house's window with the soldier himself, a teammate and a Snakeman behind him in the room, before and after; the UFO's hull and the picture without mirrors unchanged. The setting's description says "Units too". Noticed on the way, as it was before: window glass mirrors only with *Smooth light* on
- **The hit log (Ctrl+H) reads the same for the same actions on the map and in 3D** (the owner: "Check that the hit-log works properly in 3D! And iso!!", then "Hit log scrutinize and compare to iso or game without 3d! It should behave the same"). The map's log is the game's own and the add-on leaves its code alone (ActionMenuState writes the weapon's note when a use is picked from its menu; ProjectileFlyBState an arrow for each thing let fly; TileEngine the hits). Compared step by step, the same actions from the same saves on the map and in 3D, the log's whole text and the turn diary's size after each step read by a temporary trace: shots, a change of fire mode, a shot refused for want of time units, two blows, a grenade thrown, a new turn, another soldier and back, akimbo both at once and in turn, a blaster bomb's launch. Where they differed, the 3D view now does as the map: (1) two blows in a row went under one note in 3D; on the map a blow is picked anew each time, so each has its note; (2) another soldier selected and the first one back, his next shot went on under his old note in 3D; on the map selecting another soldier puts the aim down, so the next shot is a new pick and a new note (the 3D view's pick now ends with another soldier, a walk, a mode or hand switched, an item taken from the floor, and whenever the game calls its own aim off: `FirstPerson::aimCalledOff`, counted in BattlescapeGame's two cancels, after a throw or a launch and at the map's own buttons); (3) the blaster launcher's and psi's aim, which the 3D view hands to the map, wrote no note (the map's menu writes one at that pick); (4) Doom's free grenade wrote neither its note nor its arrow; (5) the other gun's shot of an arcing pair in turn wrote a note of its own (a pair's shots go under the clicked gun's note on the map). A shot the game refuses for want of time units writes its note, as the map's pick does whatever the time units (the change made the day before not to was taken back), and a blow or a launch without time units, or without a missile, writes none, as on the map. Same, and kept: shots of one pick, the arrows of a burst and of akimbo, a new turn, a throw, reaction fire (the game prints the weapon's name again after it, in either view). `FirstPersonMode::noteWeaponInHitLog` (the pick, `_hitLogPick`), `endHitLogPick`; scripts and results kept with the tools (`n23_tools/hitlog`)
- *Voxel units*: **XPiratez's own soldiers hold their weapons properly too** (the owner, on the question left open by issue #249's sweep, whether the soldiers' armours should get the enemies' cure: "Do it"). The sweep had dumped 417 soldier armours with a pistol and with a two-handed gun; they stand for 1,523 pairs of soldier type and armour. Each body was made again by the present builder as it is and with `arms: posed`, and measured: how many cubes of an arm lie away from the posed arm (the second forearm left at the hip), and what posing keeps of each arm, the trunk and the legs. **164 sprite sheets** are named `arms: posed` in the unit table: those on which the leftover shows, posing keeps seven tenths or more of each arm and all of the trunk on every body measured, and that XPiratez's soldiers alone wear. They are worn by 1,043 pairs: the Warrior, Hand, Veteran, Freak, Weirdgal and Repentia gals, heroes, slaves, purebloods, peasants, damsels, Syns, Shades, and a few gnomes and hybrids. Left out, and why: 20 sheets a Lamia or an Ogre wears (a Lamia with a pistol loses her upper body posed; 15 of 17 Ogre armours have an arm trimmed), 20 on which an arm would lose more than three tenths, 9 worn by other units or other games' units too, 6 with arm words already, 91 with nothing to cure, and the Inferna armour's (it passed by the present measure and failed the sweep's earlier one). The gals proper are untouched: they are people, made the standard humanoid, with their arms on their bones already (178 bodies on the named sheets, the same before and after). `arms: reach` was measured as well and not given: it changes the gals proper too, and only 20 of 168 pistol bodies have the arm three pixels or more under the pistol. Checked in the game: a save of sixteen soldiers dumped with the new table gives, body for body, what the offline builder had predicted (12 named bodies posed, 4 on unnamed sheets as before); 78 soldiers kneeling, dumped by the game before and after: the 4 gals proper the same, the 74 others tidied (the leftover gone on 63), their trunks, legs and heights unchanged, the least an arm keeps 65 in 100 (a hero's diving suit, whose left arm was mostly leftover)
- *Voxel units*: **two more one-tile turrets as turrets** (the owner, on the other one-tile turrets: "Do it", after the plan to build each both ways and name only those that improve). The eleven turrets of XPiratez, The X-Com Files and the 40k mod still on the general carving were dumped by the game and built both ways, each against its eight drawings as the map draws them. Named `shape: turret`: **The X-Com Files' Wizkid turret** (a drum carved 27 pixels tall against 20 drawn, its barrel lost; now the drawn height, with its barrel, and a low wreck) and **the 40k mod's Tauros small turret** (a gun carved flat as paper, six tenths of what is drawn; now a solid block with its muzzle end, still rough). Left as they are: XPiratez's blue alloy turret (no better: the barrel clearer, the dome a slab), XPiratez's small alien turret and The X-Com Files' small plasma turret (the head on its cone would become a thin pillar), the four X-Com Files roboturrets (worse, and they can move, so the word would not apply), the 40k mod's side turret (all eight drawings one picture), and The X-Com Files' Cyberwatch turret, which has no body at all (three of its eight drawings are alike: it stays its sprite). Seen in the game beside their sprites, both
- *Pixel size*, no change (the owner, asked whether Graphics detail Medium on the CPU should go back to Auto as in 1.14: "Low graphic setting pixel size 2"): Low on the CPU has Pixel size 2 already; Medium and High keep 1. Then the card's Low was given *Pixel size* 2 too (the owner: "Yes") and taken back the same night (the owner: "undo and make the gpu low to 1 pixel if it does not help"): measured on the graphics card at 1920x1080, Low drew a frame in 7.4 ms with pixels of 1 and 7.7 ms with pixels of 2, the rays being 0.4 ms of it; it only made the picture coarser. The card's Low keeps 1

- **A shot at a wall beside you no longer spins you round** (a player, GitHub issue #255, with a save: "Shoot this wall (spoiler, it spins you around)"; "can we just ENTIRELY remove the weird south east or whatever effect? Kill this bug at the source??"). The source was killed long ago, and came back by a side door. The game turns a unit to face what it shoots at, and for a point on the unit's own tile (the wall it stands against, the floor at its feet) its sum gives south; with the 3D view in the battle that turn is left out. Whether a battle has the 3D view was kept as a switch: on when a battle's screen is made, off when one closes. A save loaded during a battle makes the new battle's screen before the old one closes, so the old one switched it off a frame later, and for the rest of that battle every shot at one's own tile turned the soldier south again, the view with him. It is a count now: as many as there are battle screens with the view. Shown on the reporter's save: after a quickload, the shot's turn was to the south (direction 4 from a soldier facing north); now none, after one reload and after two.
- **A glass wall's bars run whole, its glass is flat** (the same issue, with a picture and a save: "Glass walls of this sort have their 'non collision' glass (actual collision is like a grid) recessed slightly into the wall, looks ugly. Should ATTEMPT to keep flat glass, where possible"). XPiratez's glass walls and glass doors are shaped as a grid: whole slabs two voxels thick, and between them layers with a few posts one voxel thick, so shots can pass. The view fills such openings with glass. Three things were off: the fill was glass wherever the art is inside the pane, a mullion the art draws there too, though the wall's own voxels show that mullion solid: a bar solid on the slabs and glass in the slots between them, a stack of dark boxes on the pane (the reporter's picture); where a post stood, one voxel thick, the row behind it was left empty, a pocket in the pane's other face; and the frame's own art in a slot was not filled at all. Now a slot (two voxels high at most, between the wall's own voxels) continues what is above and below it: solid where both are the frame's and the art draws a pixel, glass otherwise; and a pane is as thick as its wall wherever it has glass. All of it is a look only, as the filled glass was: shots pass where the game lets them, and it goes with the glass when the window breaks. A window's own opening is taller than a slot, so its bars stay under the smooth pane as before.
- *Wall paint*: **a wall without a window takes the other side's paint whole** (the same issue: "smeared wall"; the reporter's guess, that *Surface relief* "tunnels through from other side", was not it). A wall's face the map never shows takes the paint of the side it looks onto, but only on the voxels of its own side's main colour group: so that a window's frame and glass keep theirs. XPiratez's mirror wall is steel with glints in plain grey, two groups: from outside it was the building's plaster with the glints left on it as grey blocks, 59 of the face's 384 voxels. A wall with nothing to see through (no glass, no holes in its art) now takes the paint on all of that face; a wall with a window as before. In both renderers. (Not seen from the reporter's own save as loaded: there the doorway beside the wall is open, the room counts as outdoors when a save is loaded and nothing is painted; shown with the doorway closed, as the battle began.)
- **Display filters and the 3D view** (a player, GitHub issue #254: "video, display filters, do not apply to 3d mode"). Measured, with xBRZ and with the game's OpenGL output and a CRT shader: at *3D resolution* HUD the filter changes the 3D world as it does the map (10% of its pixels with xBRZ, 77% with the CRT shader); at Display it changes none. That is what Display means: the world is drawn at the display's own size and laid into the frame after the game has enlarged and filtered its screen, so there is nothing for an enlarging filter to work on. Nothing changed in the code: the setting's description and the guide now say it (*3D resolution*: "The game's Display Filter does not reach it at Display"). For a filter over the 3D world, choose *3D resolution* HUD.
- *Voxel units*: **hands on weapons, measured over every unit of XPiratez** (a player, GitHub issue #249, with two saves: "Arm jank ... If possible a full sweep to look for all units with arms, make sure all have proper application, it applies to ALL possible arm poses"). The sweep was made: XPiratez's 331 kinds of unit drawn with arms of their own are 281 once those drawn and named alike are counted once, and each of the 281 was put in a battle holding a pistol, then a two-handed gun, then a blade, its body made by the game and measured, 843 bodies; and 417 soldiers' armours the same with a pistol and a gun, 834 more. No arm ended short of its weapon (the builder already lays a bone to the grip); what showed, on 178 of the 241 that the table did not name yet, was the arm as drawn left beside that bone, a second forearm and hand at the hip under the gun. `arms: posed` takes it away, and the table now names the 141 sprite sheets on which it showed, posing keeps seven tenths or more of each arm, and which only XPiratez's own units wear (156 kinds of unit: bandits, traders, the armies, Humanists, government men, raiders, scientists, zombies). A second fault was the reporter's own picture, a Brute with a pistol in the air at its chest and both arms hanging to the knees: XPiratez draws its Brutes' arms long as an ape's, the one on a pistol bent short from the front and hanging from the side, and two bones of the free arm's length from shoulder to grip put the elbow at the thigh. New word `arms: reach`: such an arm hangs from the shoulder to the hand's height and reaches across to the weapon. Of the 52 of the 241 whose pistol arm hung three pixels or more under the pistol with posing alone, 46 no longer do; the table gives the word to the 32 sheets among them that only XPiratez's units wear, the Brutes and all of their build with them. Not named: sheets that other games' units or soldiers wear too, the rest of the 34 that posing trims by more than three tenths of an arm, and the soldiers' own armours (measured: most types have the leftover and posing tidies it, but a Lamia soldier with a pistol loses her upper body, and each type has armours not looked at one by one). Standing bodies only were measured, not aiming or kneeling ones.
- *Voxel units*: **the gals: chest and seat, no belly** (a player, GitHub issue #248, with a save: XPiratez's doll "seems to have its butt and boobs inverted, giving a big gut, and large back muscles"; and the owner, of every gal, with her sprite beside her body: "bulge in the belly is the problem. Make emphasized back side and breasts and no bulge at the lower belly ... Check the upper sprite and our generator"). Two rules of the generator made it, and the sprite has neither. The gals' torsos are drawn from the side up to ten pixels deep on eight wide, most of it behind the body's middle (hair, a back plate, what lies under the arm), and their depth was limited (`torsoDepth: 80`) by cutting at the front: the chest went, the trunk stood behind the hips, and the hips stuck out under it. And where a trunk meets its legs the rows between were filled out to the trunk's fullest row near its foot (made for bodies whose parts end in a point, a Sectoid's): on a gal that row is the one under the bust, and it went down as a block from chest to crotch. Now the limit trims the back, below the head only, so the chest is as far forward as drawn; and the gals' sheets are named `waist: drawn` (new word): the waist draws in under the chest as in the drawings. Measured on the 356 soldiers' armours on gals' sheets that the arm sweep had made: of the 95 gals proper, 44 had the belly further forward than the chest, now none (the chest is 1.8 pixels ahead of the lower belly in the middle one), and the seat stands 0.8 pixels behind the waist where it stood level; of the 261 other soldiers on those sheets, 88 before, 14 now (Lamias, whose "belly" is a snake's coil, and riders). The doll needs no entry of her own.
- *Voxel units*: **turrets: one barrel where one is drawn, a small turret that stands, wrecks that look like wrecks** (a player, GitHub issue #250, with a save). XPiratez's gun turrets (sheet XTURRET, and The X-Com Files' UAC turret, the same drawings) show their one barrel between two struts of the mount, which read as two muzzles: named `barrels: 1`. Its minigun turret, one tile big, a gun on a post over a ring of wire, was carved as any creature is: the post and the ring, a pixel thin, went, and the gun hung over its pedestal as a lump ("turret hovers, when it shouldn't. Also, could use barrel definition"). New word `shape: turret`: a unit one tile big that cannot move is made as the big turrets are, a plain block on the floor with its barrel, and its wreck is that block made lower and painted by its wreck's picture, where the picture laid flat on the floor was "a big metal smear". And a big animal's wreck (a unit the table names a beast or gives legs) is pressed to four tenths of its height at most: a spider queen's wreck was her own body at seven tenths, as tall on her legs as alive ("bigspiders on the map don't QUITE look dead enough"). Not done: a height limit for every corpse.
- **The hit log names the weapon fired from the 3D view** (a player, GitHub issue #251: "CTRL H and CTRL ALT H don't function correctly in first person? notably, no note for what gun"). The game writes the weapon into its hit log when a fire mode is picked from the weapon's menu on the map; a shot or a blow from the 3D view has no menu, so its hits stood in the log (Ctrl+H) and the turn's diary (Ctrl+Alt+H) under no weapon, or under the one last picked on the map. The 3D view now writes that note itself: a new one when the soldier, the weapon, the fire mode, his tile or the turn has changed, or the log no longer begins with this weapon; otherwise shot after shot goes under the note there is, as on the map while a fire mode stays picked.
- *Voxel units*: **the Bullfrog sits on the ground, with a frog's back** (a player, GitHub issue #252, with a save: "frogs in piratez derpy. Also, slightly off ground"). XPiratez's Bullfrog is The X-Com Files' own, and had its rule already (`sculpt: sides`): cut so, its feet go, its belly hung three voxels over the ground, and its four drawings, all made from above, left a point on its back. New word `stands: floor`: a body the game stands on the ground is drawn as much lower as its lowest cube is over the floor (what a shot meets comes down with it). And the frogs are named `flat: 60`, their backs pressed lower. It is still a rough frog.
- **A swimming pool mirrors** (a player, GitHub issue #253, with a save: "could use reflective water effect"). XPiratez's pool is four floors of its city decor, which the table of still water did not name; and named, they still would not have mirrored: the game gives a pool's water no voxels at all, the 3D view fills such a floor in flat from its picture, and the still-water rule was only ever asked of floors with voxels. Both now: the table names the four floors, and the same four pictures in five more terrain sets of XPiratez's and The X-Com Files' that the scan for same pictures found (`ARENA`, `DECORLUX`, `MADDECOR`, `XB3BITZ3`, `HAVIRABASE`), and a flat floor without voxels that the table names mirrors as a pond does. The same report's "glass was off because shadows from every light was off" was not found: on the reporter's save the window glass mirrors with *Shadows from every light* off as with it on (it needs *True colour* and *Reflections*).
- **Metallic surfaces** (the owner: "Could we add a metallic map to metal surfaces like ufo hulls? This would be great as a first next version feature", "Make for ufo hulls at the beginning"). New setting *Metallic surfaces* (Off / Sheen / Mirror; off by default; *Renderer* GPU; in the SCREEN group). The 3D view already knows which tiles are a UFO's (*UFO lights and aura* goes by it), and *Surface relief* already tilts a face by its drawing; with the setting a UFO's parts answer the light as metal does. *Sheen*: a glint of the sun (a highlight on the face turned halfway between the sun and the eye, on the tilted face where *Surface relief* is on), the sky's light on a hull looked along, and the lamps' light (flashlight, flares) on a face turned to the eye; a highlight brightens a colour within its own group, by three and a half shades at most and no more than half the shades it has left to its group's brightest (the first try, six shades of sun and five of room, turned a pale hull panel into a white patch). *Mirror*: with *Reflections*, one ray off the metal's face, what it meets or the sky, tinted by the hull's colour and mixed in by 12% head on to 35% along the face; in two goes with the other mirrors, so only a hull's pixels pay for it. `MetalShine`, `MetalSun`, `MetalRim`, `MetalLamp`, `MetalBrightest`, `MetalRoom`, `MetalMirror` in FirstPersonViewShared.h; the shader's `METAL` (a program of its own, as `RELIEF` is: with the setting off the card's program is as it was).
- *Voxel units*: **spiders drawn with thicker legs keep their legs** (a player, GitHub issue #247: "spiders, which are pallet swaps, do not retain legs"). The X-Com Files' purple, red, green and alloy giant spiders are not its plain one in other colours: they are drawn anew with legs two pixels thick, and the rule for creatures on thin legs (the unit table's `legs: N`) took legs that thick for body: a lump on a stalk. Where the front drawing shows no thin pixels beside the body at the usual thickness, the rule now tries the next ones up, to twice as thick, until the legs stand apart from a body that is still there (`legThin`). A unit whose legs were found before is made exactly as before.
- **The polar ice's open water mirrors, and two more waters** (the same issue: "Water, which was made shiny in piratez, is not working in XCF despite being the EXACT same texture"). True of the picture, not of the name: the snow swamp's and the beach's water are X-COM's own polar water, pixel for pixel, and the table of still water (`stillWater:`) named those sets and not `POLAR`. A scan of every terrain set of the five games for floors whose picture is exactly a named water floor's (a developer's tool) found three: `POLAR` (its water and shore floors, records 61 to 80: in X-COM itself too), XPiratez's `LAVA_SWAMP` and the sea's `SEABITS`. All three are in the table now; with *Reflections* on the GPU renderer they mirror as the swamps do.
- **A scorch on the ground is whole from any distance, and when you stand on it** (the owner, with a photo: "the dark explosion patches on the ground are cut off at a certain distance (is this connected to view distance?)"). Not the view distance: a shade on the ground (scorched earth, a unit's shadow) darkens only the pixels that show the floor right there, and that was asked along the view, within 3 voxels. Looked at from a soldier's eye, low and far, the same floor half a voxel under the shade's plane is many voxels further off along the ray, the more the further away, so a scorch stopped at a straight line a few tiles ahead, and a unit's shadow was gone beyond some seven tiles. Now it is asked by height: within 2 voxels of the ground's height (`GroundShadeHeight`), on both renderers. And a shade with a corner of its box behind the eye was not drawn at all (a scorch vanished when one stood on its rim): its box is then the whole view (`groundShadeBox`).
- *Voxel units*, a player's last list before a release (GitHub issue #245):
  - **Kneeling soldiers sit low** ("shots at a flat angle will go DIRECTLY into the skull of the forward unit ... I should be able to reliably fire over a kneeling units head"; "lowering the torso so the butt sits on (even partially merges with) the heel"). The unit table's new word `kneel: low`, said of every unit: a kneeling body's trunk, arms and weapon come straight down, in whole cubes, until its hips are two voxels over the ground; its legs stay as they were, the raised knee before the chest. X-COM's soldier, 21.9 voxels tall kneeling as drawn (24.9 standing), is 15.9 now; TFTD's 13.9 to 14.9: the height the games' rules give a kneeling unit (14 of 22), under a standing soldier's line of fire (18). What is hit is what is seen. `kneel: drawn` brings the drawings' height back for a unit, or for all by taking the first entry out.
  - **Arms of four more kinds of people** ("remaining arm residue / jank", "bad arm paint"; an earlier report's dark, patched arms on the 40k mod's Eldar): The X-Com Files' Church of Dagon (a disciple's pistol arm stood beside the arm as drawn), its Red Dawn, the 40k mod's Eldar and XPiratez's two punk outfits have their arms on their bones, elbow down under a hand that holds a gun up, painted by their own drawings along their length (a sleeve, then a bare hand). By name, as every such rule: tried on every kind of unit drawn as the soldier is, the same rule made the 40k mod's marines' arms too long, so it is not general.
  - **The Gilldog's head** ("no big dopey ears, just a big fat head plz"): its muzzle, two rows under its belly in the side drawing, was taken for a pair of legs a row long, as far apart as its forelegs. For a body named `trunk: round`, what hangs under the belly without reaching the ground is of the head or the tail, and nothing stands apart as ears.
  - **The big sea creatures move** ("2x2 animations for xarquid and halucinoid"): TFTD's Xarquid has a body for each of its sprite's four frames, its shell and arms writhing as the sprite's do (`animation: sprite`, which a unit two tiles by two gets only by name). The Hallucinoid, a jellyfish, beats: one body that rises, sinks and swells in a slow rhythm, each in its own step (`animation: pulse`, new). Its eight frames, each made a body by itself, came out a dome, then a diamond, in other colours each; what a shot meets rises and sinks with the body.
  - **XMbl prefers the far light** ("make far light preference for XMBL"): choosing the Gameplay preset XMbl sets *Flashlight* to Far light, as it sets *Pixel size* to 1; a profile already on XMbl keeps its flashlight.
  - **No slot in the back** ("slot in back on SS troopers", and an earlier report's "segmented back slot" on the 40k mod's Eldar): where a head of hair, a crest or a collar stands out behind the neck and a jacket or a pack stands out lower down, the nape between them was as deep as the side drawings have the neck: a channel across the back under the hair, three voxels high and a voxel and a half deep on XPiratez's slave soldier in the punk outfit. The unit table's new word `back: smooth` fills such a hollow of the trunk, behind the body's middle, up to four voxels high; said of XPiratez's soldiers (names with PIR) and the Eldar.
  - **The bloodhound's missing leg** ("blood hound strange leg"; "this will apply to gildog in XCF most likely, these are literally pallet swaps"): its muzzle, hanging under the chest in the side drawing, was taken for a second foreleg, the left one, a stump, and the real forelegs for the right one alone. The hounds drawn as the Gilldog is (XPiratez's blood dog and bloodhound soldier, sheet BAN_6.PCK; The X-Com Files' bloodhound) are named `trunk: round` with it: a round trunk, one head, four legs on the ground.
  - **A ghost leaves no body** ("a ghost gal ... dies, and leaves a 3d corpse, despite none in iso"): a corpse whose own picture is blank, as XPiratez's ghosts', is not laid out as a voxel body either.
  - **The Eldar's Howling Banshee is no ghost**, and its Striking Scorpion no animal (names the table's words BANSHEE and SCORPION caught).
  - **A big unit's wreck lies the way the unit faced** ("2x2 corpses inherit units direction on death (they always face toward iso screen)"): as a one-tile body does, by the way it faced when last seen well (the game turns a dying unit to the screen). One destroyed unseen, or before the battle was loaded, lies as the map shows it.
  - **Facing icons** ("make an ALT direction indicator like the downed unit icons : wire frame under foot is unreliable to see, depending on height and distance"). New setting **Facing icons** (Advanced, HUD; on): with *Show facing* held, an arrow on each unit, at a fixed size on the screen, as seen from above with you at the bottom.
  - **Hover sparks by name** ("hover particles off by default ... I can't be arsed to go through which should, and shouldn't have it"): every big unit that flies had them, a Xarquid and a Hallucinoid in the sea, a mod's gate or demon. The unit table's new word `hover: sparks` gives them to saucers and hover vehicles alone (names with CYBERDISC, a word ending in DISC, HOVERTANK, a word starting with HOVER, DISPLACER); nothing else has them.
  - The unit table also takes a name of one star, `"*"`, for every unit, and `routine:N` for the units the game draws with routine N.
- **A right click opens the door you point at** (a player, GitHub issue #244: "right click open doors if pointing at them ... greater iso parity, I see no reason why it shouldn't, if you are directly on the door"). New setting **Right click opens doors** (Advanced, Movement; on): with nothing to cancel, a right click on a closed door under the crosshair that is within the soldier's reach opens it, as the *Open door* key does and as a right click does on the map; any other right click is as before (a marked target forgotten, a picked action cancelled, the zoom while held).
- *Voxel units*: **turrets and tanks have barrels: round, flat at the mouth, hollow** (the owner: "Do turrets in general and do barrels. Artificially make one or two voxel barrels (depending on the turret) and join them to the turret body at place indicated where sprite says it is a turret barrel. And yes turret barrels should be hollow to look like proper tank barrels"; a player, GitHub issue #233: "barrel breach being more defined (round looking black opening, and barrel ends being flatter"). A turret (a unit two tiles by two that cannot move: the 40k mod's Chimera turret, XPiratez's truck and tank turrets) was a plain block with its gun a slab or a lump on its face; a tank's turret (UFO's cannon, laser and plasma tanks) had a round bar. Now the gun is read from the drawings and made anew. *Where and how long*: the thin end of the two side drawings, the columns from the front inwards drawn no higher than half the tallest (two or more, no more than half the drawing's length); its height is the middle of those columns' rows. *How many and how thick*: from the frontal drawing, whose gun is its rows from the bottom up no wider than half its widest row: two barrels where the lowest of them is drawn in two runs (two muzzles side by side), each as thick as its run is wide; one otherwise, as thick as those rows at their middle one; a stub drawn on the turret's face is one barrel on the middle line. Never thicker than the side drawing has the gun. *Made*: what the turret had ahead of the gun's root goes; each barrel is a round tube from inside the turret to the gun's end as drawn, cut flat, hollow behind a wall a third of its radius thick, the bore dark; it stands as high as drawn, moved only as far as it must to meet the turret (and never sunk into the hull under it). Its skin is the side drawing's gun, column for column. A tank's turret turned from its hull has its barrel turned with it (it had none then). One maker serves every turret and tank. Units whose drawings show no such thin end keep their shape (UFO's rocket tank, the 40k mod's ball turret on its pedestal) unless the unit table gives them barrels: its new word `barrels:` sets the count by name (0: none), and a turret with no gun to read then gets barrels of a plain size on its front (a fifth of its length long, three tenths of its height thick, at its middle height), so any turret of any mod can be given them; UFO's Hovertank/Launcher, whose gun is drawn as a ring on its turret, is named so. Every other body is as it was (rebuilt from kept dumps and compared).
- **Long weapons with a slot frame drawn into their inventory picture** (a player, GitHub issue #235: "item distorted, seemingly, items which exceed the window size act funny"). A picture taller than the hand slot (32x48) hangs out under it on the inventory screen, and the 40k mod (58 pictures of long weapons) draws the screen's grid anew round it: the slot's old bottom line painted over in black, a new frame in the grid's colour down the picture's sides and along its bottom. The 3D view gives an item's inventory picture volume, so the frame was a wire loop round the butt of a weapon in a body's hands, and the weapon in your own hand was cut at the slot's height and ended in a black slab (the painted line). Now the frame and the painted lines are found (both side columns in one colour from under the slot down to a row mostly of that colour) and left out, and the weapon in hand and thrown items take the picture at its full size, as bodies' items did. Pictures without such a frame are read as before.
- **Emergency climb, a key** (the owner: "a bindable key - emergency climb (50 TUs ...) sometimes units can get stuck in level depressions with no way out", "can also climb over a fence if free space tile is available on other side"). For the maps where the rules leave a unit no way out: a sunken railway track, a ditch, a yard fenced all round. The key has none at first (the game's Controls, *Emergency climb*). Its cost is a share of the unit's time units (a player's idea, taken up by the owner: "half of tu, plus 1% tu for every unit of weight %. No scaling for the overweight", so that a fast animal cannot hop fence after fence): half of its full bar with nothing carried, rising evenly with its load (what it carries against its strength) to the whole bar at a full load, and no more than that for a unit carrying over its strength; a unit with a fresh turn can always climb (what is reserved for a shot is kept). Straight along the grid the way the view looks, and only where the plain step that way is refused: *up a ledge* onto the next tile on the same level; *over a fence*, a wall between the two tiles or something standing on the next tile (then onto the tile beyond it), up to 20 voxels high, or taller if sight passes it and nothing is overhead on either side (a tall wire fence; a house's window lets sight through too, but stands under a floor: not climbed); *out of a pit*, onto the next tile a level up, when it has a floor and nothing is over the unit's own head (a low flat roof beside the unit is climbed the same way: the owner, "Roofs; yes"). The tile climbed onto must have a floor and be free of units and of anything that stops walking. The unit is set there facing the way it climbed, is seen and may be shot at as after a step (the game's reaction fire), and the camera glides over in an arc. "NOTHING TO CLIMB HERE" where there is nothing, or the way is open. Units one tile big; not with Doom IDCLIP (which needs none).
- **A door struck down is gone, texture and all: checked, and one thing fixed** (the owner: "check that punched doors really disappear along with their textures (players sometimes describe the wall texture was there while they walked through)"). Checked on both renderers with fists that break a door at one blow: a house's door (no destroyed version) and a shop's sliding door (one with remains) are gone from the picture the moment the game removes them, the view then shows what the battle loaded anew shows, and the soldier walks through. Every door of UFO, TFTD, XPiratez, The X-Com Files and the 40k mod was looked at with its destroyed version (some 1,500 doors): none leaves a wall a unit walks through; what they leave is rubble. Fixed on the way: the remains of a door that a unit can walk through show only what their art draws. Some sets give the remains a slab of the door's own shape, and its voxels behind no art took the nearest colour: a low sill of made-up texture across the doorway. What is still seen in a doorway walked through, by design: an open sliding door as a ghost (setting *Open doors as ghosts*).
- **A flashlight that reaches** (a player, GitHub issue #232: a flashlight as an old shooter had it, "a tac flare like ... effect on where a hypothetical bullet would impact, EXACTLY the same regardless of distance ... it makes the flashlight usable at virtually any distance"). New setting **Flashlight** (Advanced, Graphics; **Beam** by default: as ever, a spot from the weapon that fades with range and ends at 20 tiles). **Far light**: the light stands where the crosshair's line first meets the world, a pool six tiles across as bright at any range, found anew every frame; the line meeting nothing (the sky, past the map's edge), no light. Both renderers; with "Shadows from every light" it throws shadows as the beam does.
- *Voxel units*: **a unit keeps its colours over ground of its own colour, through night vision and in lit smoke** (a player, GitHub issues #242 "smokey ghosts" and #243 "night vision on many units shows green terrain through them", with saves). One fault: in full colour (*True colour*) a pixel that still had its ray's palette colour was shown in the ray's full colour, also where a body had been drawn over the ray in that same colour: the body took the ground's light there (through night vision its green, in patches), and under lit smoke the light of the wall behind it, and stood as a ghost. A pixel anything solid was drawn over is no longer the ray's, whatever its colour.
- *Voxel units*: **a unit hit earlier in the battle has its colours** (a player, GitHub issue #241, "smoke silhouette trigger error ... this was after a load": a Boomosaurus as one pale shape). No smoke: XPiratez's hit effect recolours a unit's sprite at every frame up to three past its last hit, and a body is made at a frame where the recolouring is at rest, looked for among the battle's first frames: all of them in that flash for a unit hit since. The frame is now looked for from the present on, and found again when it stops being at rest.
- **Swamp water has no pits** (a player, GitHub issue #238: "some frames of animations remove tiles"). A floor of still water was told by each animation frame's own art, and its face filled in by the first frame's: in some frames of its ripples the deep water was the game's five columns, a pit. A floor that animates is water in every frame if its first frame is, its face filled in in each.
- **Fire costs a tenth** (a player, GitHub issue #239: "fire in excessive amounts kills frames"). Each burning tile made its own dissolve of the same two flame frames and sent it to the card by its bytes: 17 ms a frame with two thousand tiles burning. The dissolve is made once a frame for each pair and found by its address: 1.3 ms, the same picture.
- *Voxel units*: **an arm that holds a gun up has its elbow down** (a player, GitHub issue #240: "arm is at awkward, tense angle (elbow behind back) ... elbow should be facing almost straight down, with forearm up to hold gun"). A new word for the units the table names, `arms: elbow` (with `posed`): where the arm's drawing ends 2 voxels or more below the hand on its item, that end is the elbow. Given to people of the human race (`HUMAN`); every other body the same cube for cube.
- *Voxel units*: **cat girls and gnomes stand on their legs** (a player, GitHub issue #237, "amputees", with two saves). XPiratez's cat girls have their tail drawn in the legs' frames, and it was taken for a second leg seen from the side: both legs came out in pieces off the hips and were dropped. New word `tail: drawn` (sheets `XNEKO`): what the side drawing has beside the legs is the tail, behind the body's middle. Its gnomes are drawn on the gals' sheets, and the gals' `sculpt: frontal` cut them down to one thread of a leg: `GNOME` is `sculpt: all`.
- *Voxel units*: **the Academy drone is a disc with its visor in front; the Cyberdisc bulges a little** (a player, GitHub issue #236: "the blue paint on the underside, when it should be a frontal visor", "cyberdisks should still bulge a little on top / bottom"). `ADRONE`: `flat: 60`, `flatUnder: 50`, and a new word, `paint: hull` (a body drawn on one frame is painted as a vehicle's hull is, each cube by the drawing that looks onto it most squarely). `CYBERDISC`: 50 and 60 (were 35 and 50).
- **IDCLIP for big units** (a player, GitHub issue #234: "2x2 units dont idclip"). A unit two tiles by two walks and floats through anything with Doom IDCLIP as a soldier does; each of its four tiles must be on the map and free of other units.
- *Voxel units*: **spiders have eight legs, and an animal's corpse is as long as the animal** (a player, GitHub issue #231: spiders "still have no legs", and "some corpse items ... like spiders ... seem to MASSIVELY deflate on death"). Legs a pixel thin are drawn wherever the artist had room, and the eight drawings together leave none of them. New word `legs: N` (`SPIDER`, `*SPIDER`: 8): the body is made from the thick part of its drawings, and N legs are made anew round it, as far out, as high at the knee and as thick as the front and side drawings show them, painted as the front drawing's legs. And a unit the table names a beast or gives legs lies, dead, as long as it was long or broad, not as it stood tall: a spider twelve voxels long was a corpse of six.
- **Four things from the 40k mod** (a player, GitHub issue #233, with four saves). *Units under a low roof*: a unit is cut off at the floor over its head; one on a raised deck under a low roof (a transport's) was a stump, and is now drawn whole where the cut would leave less than 65 in 100 of its height ("if a unit is, say, above 35% 'reduced' under a roof ... just draw completely"). *Lines along a floor's and a wall's edge* ("texture is meant to be a GRID, but seemingly 2 parts are omitted"): a floor's top takes its art's own edge pixels along its west, south and east edges where they are a line of another colour, and a thin wall drawn flat reads its art as on the tile's edge through all its thickness (the face turned to the room showed it shifted, its top line gone). *Deep space*: the mod's ships (`STR_DEEPSPACE_ENVIRO`) have the space sky, and no rock round them. *A gun on a mount*: a unit that cannot move and is drawn the same from three or more of its eight sides (a plate bolted to a vehicle, its gun drawn where it can point) has no body and is drawn as its picture. Not done: more detail on turrets' barrels (a turret is a plain block by the owner's ruling).
- **Two descriptions: Surface relief warns, Renderer fits** (the owner: "Surface relief - add a warning different colour! 'Takes some time!!' (Similar as for voxel units)", "Renderer description is too long. Shorten it (take out also linux windows macos....). Fit it."). A setting's description has two lines of 305 pixels under the list; what is longer is cut and ends in three dots. *Renderer*'s took four lines, so its warning never showed: now "CPU: runs anywhere. GPU: the graphics card draws the 3D world; the CPU takes over where it can't." and, as the warning, "The first switch takes a while." *Surface relief* ends in the warning "Takes some time!" (the card's programs are made again when it is switched). A warning is the part of a description after `{ALT}`, drawn in the text's second colour: in the options opened from the main menu that is another colour already; in the options opened in a battle the battle's colours gave both parts one colour, so there the 3D VIEW tab now gives the warning the headings' gold (`OptionsFirstPersonState::init`), for *Voxel units*' warning too. New local tool `tools/testing/desc_lines.py`: how a description wraps in those two lines, measured with the game's small font as the game wraps it; by it 18 more descriptions of the tab were longer than two lines and cut in the game (the tactical map's and VR foveation's at five lines the longest). Asked, the owner: "Yes, shorten all 18". All 18 are rewritten to two lines, each keeping its point (*Tactical map*, *Compass: step costs*, *Jump over low obstacles*, *Sidesteps face the view*, *Reflections*, *Rangefinder*, *Tactical map: wheel changes level*, *HUD scale*, *Hit sounds*, *Open doors as ghosts*, *Close range: from the eye*, and the seven of VR); what they left out is in this manual's settings. All 201 descriptions of the tab now fit, in en-US and en-GB, and the suite's new step 299 keeps it so.
- **The tactical map's wheel changes the level, the map keeps its zoom, one key one control, "PREPARING 3D..."** (the owner, four asks in one message). *The wheel* ("for minimap the wheel should rather change levels of map and not zoom"): *Tactical map: wheel changes level* is YES by default (Ctrl and the wheel zoom, and the + and - keys and buttons as ever); a profile from before, written with the old default, is given it once, with the V key above (`fpsKeyDefaults` 2), and set back by hand it stays the player's. *The zoom* ("minimap should remember your previously set zoom level"): it was kept while the game ran and forgotten when it was left; now it is kept with the player's options (the unlisted `fpsMapZoom`), so the next battle and the next day's game open the map as it was left. *One key, one control* ("if you bind the same key - it should unbind elsewhere ... now it stays bound to multiple things"): a key bound in the 3D VIEW tab's Controls list is taken from every other control of that list that had it, the add-on's own and the game's that work in 3D; the other row shows empty at once (`lstOptionsKeyPress`). The game's keys of its other screens are not touched: the same key may mean something else on the Geoscape. *The notice* while the graphics card gets ready at a battle's start: "PREPARING 3D..." (it was "PREPARING THE 3D VIEW"). Checked in the suite's step 298 (11 checks): the wheel's default, the once and the left-alone case; C bound to the view's second key taken from the mouse mode's, saved so; the map two steps in, the same at the next start of the game.
- **V is the 3D view's key on every profile; the auto shot has no key** (the owner: "The V key should be bound to 3d view by default and auto key unbound"). It was so on a new profile only: V is the second key for the isometric / 3D switch (beside F8) and the auto shot has none, the wheel and the next fire mode key reaching it. A profile from before had the auto shot on V, and the rule made for it kept the auto shot there and left the switch without a second key: so on every profile that had played before, V still picked the auto shot. Now, once for each profile (the unlisted option `fpsKeyDefaults`; `settleOptions`, at the first battle or the first look at the 3D VIEW tab): a profile with the auto shot on V has no key on the auto shot, and V on the switch where the switch has no second key. After that the two keys are the player's: the auto shot put back on V by hand stays there. Both bound to one key by hand, the key is the switch's. Checked (the suite's step 298): a new profile; a profile from before, at its first battle, and V then going from the map to the 3D view; the auto shot put back on V by hand, left alone.
- **Realistic hitboxes; XMbl's smooth light and fine pixels** (the last two points of the same list, GitHub issue #193; the owner: "Yes make both"). The setting *Full sprite hitboxes* is named **Realistic hitboxes** (the player: "should be rename realistic hitbox"); its option is still `fpsSpriteHitboxes`, and the history below keeps the old name. XMbl, and FullQoL and FPS after it, make the light smooth between tiles (*Smooth lighting*; the first three presets leave it as the player has it), and XMbl, when it is chosen, sets *Pixel size* to 1, full detail. The pixel size is *Graphics detail*'s (2 at Low on the CPU), so it is set on the choice only and is no part of what makes XMbl: a player on Low, or with a size of their own, is on XMbl all the same, and *Graphics detail* says Custom once XMbl has changed its pixel size. For this a preset's table can leave a setting as it is (`Kept`) and can list one that is only set on a choice (`PresetSwitch::onChoice`); choosing a preset of one group lets the other group's name follow where they share a setting (`choosePreset`). Checked in the suite's step 297: Extended+ leaves smooth lighting off where the player has it off; XMbl chosen from the CPU's Low gives smooth lighting, a pixel size of 1 and *Graphics detail* Custom; a pixel size of 2 by hand after is XMbl still; smooth lighting off by hand is Custom.
- **Presets and defaults: the owner's rulings on a player's list** (GitHub issue #193, "Preset adjustments": some thirty of the game's own options the player would switch on in every preset but FAITHFUL, changes to three presets, two defaults). Ruled, one question each. *The game's own options*: no preset touches them; a row of its own on the first page of the 3D VIEW tab, **Recommended game options** (Apply, then Applied; `fpsGameOptions`, `FirstPerson::applyGameOptions`), sets the player's list when clicked: the Geoscape and the Battlescape at 640x400; on the globe the countries, radars and flight paths, the funds shown, the middle button to drag there and on the map; the path's arrows with their time units, tooltips; info screens maximized, values by the wheel (10); the building queue, crafts launched whatever their state, crafts transferred in the air, the globe's realistic light; inventory stats, grenades saved primed, explosion height 2, auto-end battle, the alternate movement methods, the line of fire overridden, the smooth bullet camera, no "Next Turn" screen; OXCE's links, clip sizes, a craft's maintenance time (2), the base's name in popups, the base's large bars scaled, the accuracy by the crosshair (2). As any change on the options screens it is kept with OK and dropped with Cancel; an option a mod fixes for its players stays as the mod has it. *The presets* (`gameplaySwitches`): *Downed unit icons* Always from Extended+ on (FAITHFUL keeps Crosshair); XMbl gets *Lean* Tilt, *Jump over low obstacles* and *Fall damage*. Not taken: the compass's step costs in Extended+, plain hitboxes in XMbl. *Auto-equip guns first*: on by default and in all six presets. *Wall paint*: stays a setting; All rooms is the default and every preset's value. A profile that played before would have gained nothing: its settings, as its preset had them before, no longer made that preset and would have shown as Custom with the old values. So once (`fpsPresetTables`, unlisted; `settlePresets`): settings that are all as a named preset had them before the change become that preset as it is now; a mix of the player's own matches no preset's old values and is left alone, and so are the old values chosen again by hand afterwards. Checked: a new profile's defaults; a profile on the old Extended+ carried over, once; the two cases left alone; XMbl chosen by clicks; the row clicked and kept with OK (the suite's step 297, with steps 200, 225, 287, 288 and 295).
- *Voxel units*: **the Cyberdisc is a disc, the Hallucinoid's bell is round** (a player, GitHub issue #226: "discs to be more 'disk' shaped, and halucanoid tops being slightly flatter"). Both are drawn from above in all eight directions, and what eight such drawings leave of a flat thing is a cone on its top and one under it, each a third of its width high: the saucer was a spinning top, 9 voxels of cone on its rim. Two words of the unit table, for the units named and no others: `flat:` and `flatUnder:` (see "Units by name"; `pressFlat`). What stands above the body's widest level is pressed to that many hundredths of its height and rounded off, a level taking the place of one further out (pressed to half or less a cone becomes a dome, pressed less its point is blunted); what hangs under it likewise. Only ever down towards the widest level: nothing stands outside what the drawings show, and the top's colours come nearer to where the drawing has them (a saucer's top is flat). `CYBERDISC`: 35 and 50; `HALLUCINOID`: 70. Its hits and its wreck are the pressed body's. Looked at in the game, both; 634 other test bodies cube for cube the same.
- *Voxel units*: **no flat picture while a unit aims** (a player, GitHub issue #228: "2x2 units / muckstars (units with no rotations) flash sprite in voxel mode when reaction firing"). A body was kept under how its unit stands, aiming or not, and a unit aiming for the first time had a body made for that; until it was made the unit showed as its sprite. The game draws every unit two tiles by two, and those drawn the same from every side (The X-Com Files' Muckstar), the same whether they aim or not: their second body was the first one over again, and a big one takes long to make. Measured on a Cyberdisc, a Sectopod, a Reaper and a hover tank firing at a soldier walking past: 131 frames as flat pictures between them. Now aiming is part of a body's key only for the units the game draws otherwise while they aim (`aimingShows`: the bodies in parts, routine 4's kin, the Silacoid): the others keep their one body, 0 frames as pictures in the same walk. And any unit whose body for how it is now is still being made (a soldier's first shot, a first kneel, another weapon in hand) keeps the body it was last drawn with meanwhile, not its sprite (`VoxelUnits::modelFor`).
- **Shot marks are as big in the world from any distance** (a player, GitHub issue #230, of a heavy plasma's scorch: "the hole appears big ... with distance, and like a pinhole up close"). A mark was drawn no more than 6 pixels from its middle however near: the same dot on the screen from one tile away as from three, and the finer the 3D view's resolution the smaller. A bullet hole is 0.6 voxels across and a scorch 1.8, and now they are that from any distance: only right against one is it held to a quarter of the view's height from its middle (`MarkMostOfView`), so it never fills the view; far away it is still at least its one pixel. The mark is centred where the shot's line meets what is drawn, not on a voxel's corner: looked at up close after the change. On both renderers.
- *See-through corpses*: **a body you look past stays solid** (a player, GitHub issue #227: "Corpses in voxel mode become transparent when not looking at voxel corpse. Acts as if using living hitbox?"). The same fault as the wreck's below (#222), on a small body, reported from a build before that fix: a lying body counted as in the way of any aim passing within 12 voxels of the floor near it, the height of a box, not of the body. Checked on a downed soldier three tiles ahead with the crosshair before it, on it and at twelve places past it: solid every time. It is drawn faint only before a unit standing behind it, or where the aim's line really goes through it to something a tile or more behind.
- *Voxel units*: **a kneeling person kneels: legs from bones, down on one knee** (the owner: "Make kneeling beautiful"; a player, GitHub issue #225, with pictures of a kneeling soldier, asked for the body pressed lower by a forward lean: not done, a body keeps the size and the pose of its drawings). The standard humanoid left a kneeling body's legs as cut: from eight small drawings a kneeling pair of legs is one lump, smoothed layer by layer into two stumps under a flat disc of hips. Now the legs of a kneeling person (the units the table names `humanoid: standard`, drawn in parts) are made from bones, as arms are (`standardBody`, steps 4b and 7b): the hips at the trunk's foot; on the side whose legs reach less far back on the floor the knee is up, the thigh ahead to it, as far ahead and as high as the lump is there, the shin down to a foot flat on the floor (the standing body's foot, as long as when it stands); on the other side the thigh goes down to a knee on the floor and the shin back along the floor to a foot on its toes, as far back as the lump reaches. How far out the knees and feet stand is the lump's breadth on the floor. A thigh is 62 in 100 of the trunk's half breadth thick, half, and no more than 7.5 in 100 of the body's height (measured standing: 1.45 to 1.7 voxels); the shin as thin as the lump is between foot and knee, within 55 to 100 in 100 of the thigh (a power suit's boot is as thick as its thigh). Colours: the legs by their own drawing, where that has no pixel the nearest it has (a knee held up before the body took the colours of the arm and weapon drawn over it); the foot behind by its own pixel of the back drawing along the views' slant, as the foot ahead by the front's. Looked at before and after from six sides: UFO's soldier in coveralls, personal armour, power suit and flying suit, TFTD's in its four, a soldier of XPiratez and one of The X-Com Files; in the game the four of UFO. On every one the left knee is up, as drawn. Proved unchanged: 324 other bodies of the four games built by the builder before and after (standing soldiers in every armour, aliens, civilians, big units, kneeling units not named), cube for cube; the 30 that differ are the kneeling people.
- *Reflections*: **window glass mirrors so that it shows** (a player, GitHub issue #224: "Windows/ glass not reflecting? I don't think I've noticed it, not once"). A pane mirrored 8 in 100 of what is in front of it seen straight on, more only from 60 degrees aside, under a flat pale blue of 30 in 100: in play it did not show. Now a fifth of the pane is the mirrored picture seen straight on, rising with the square of the angle to 85 in 100 along the pane (`GlassMirror`, `GlassMirrorMost`), and with *Reflections* on the pane's flat pale blue is half as strong (15 in 100): the mirrored picture is that now. A mirrored face takes the light of the side it is seen from, a voxel and a half before it, as a face seen straight does: at the face itself a room's far wall took the light of the tile behind it, the night outside, and a lit room mirrored dark. Through night-vision goggles too, in their colours (it was off there). Seen in a lit room at dusk (the bed and the floor in both panes), through the goggles, and from the street by day (a shop's window with the street in it, small panes with the sky). Units, and the soldier whose eyes these are, are not in the mirrored picture: it is one ray through the terrain. The sea and still water are unchanged.
- *Voxel units*: **a knife is a knife: melee weapons that share a hand drawing** (a player, GitHub issue #223: "smaller weapons are scaled up ... The shiv is even worse, I didn't even recognize it (the 1x1 knife, it looked like a sword"). What a body holds is its inventory picture given volume, made as long as its hand drawing shows it. The mods draw several weapons in the hand with one drawing: XPiratez its shiv, one inventory square, with its machete's. Now a melee weapon that shares its hand drawing with longer ones is as much shorter than the drawing as its inventory picture (its drawn box, corner to corner) is shorter than the longest among them, 35 in 100 at the least (`VoxelUnits::handShare`, `HeldItem::share`); it is shortened from the hand that holds it, which stays where the drawing has it. XPiratez: the shiv 59 in 100 of its drawing, the machete 92, an axe 92, a cutlass whole. The games' own melee weapons each have a hand drawing of their own: all 54 bodies of the two test battles hold their items as before; of an X-Com Files battle's 42, two change (87 and 96 in 100). Guns are left as they are.
- *Voxel units*: **a wreck looked at is solid** (a player, GitHub issue #222: "a combination of, I think night vision, 2x2, and explosion makes a unit ghostly?", a Reaper seen through with its far side showing). It was the Reaper's wreck after the blast, made see-through by *See-through corpses*: a body lying in the way of the aim is drawn faint. "In the way" was: the aim's line passes within 18 voxels of a wreck on the ground, under 20 voxels up, and ends beyond it: nearly any aim a little downwards near a wreck, the crosshair on the wreck itself too, whatever the wreck's height. Now a lying body or a wreck is see-through before a unit standing behind it, as ever; else only where the aim's line really goes through it, under the top its shape has where the line leaves its reach, and ends a tile or more past that (`VoxelCorpsePast`). Looking at a wreck, or over it at what lies behind, it stays solid. Reproduced with a dead Cyberdisc ahead of a soldier looking over it at a fence: a ghost before, solid now. From further off, pointing at the top of a tall wreck still shows through it: there the aim does pass through it to the ground well beyond.
- *Voxel units*: **one arm an arm, a head of face and helmet, a round Gilldog: three words for named units** (a player, GitHub issue #221, with a save: "units in particular poses have 'multiple' arms? I presume this is because the older, flawed arm code is still running, and leaves leftovers?", The X-Com Files' Gilldog "has a very STRANGE head ... it should probably assume a round shape"; the owner: "Delicately and one by one check save before and after!", and of two units on a sheet: "again bald spot - keep only the face and colour top of head like hair or helmet!"). Each is a word of the unit table, on for the units named and no others. `arms: posed` (the Deep Ones, TFTD's and The X-Com Files'; the human race of The X-Com Files and XPiratez): the player was right. An item is placed by how it is held, pointing ahead, and the arm's bones go to it; the arm as its drawings have it, bent at the hip or hanging, stayed beside that one. Now, of the drawn arm, only what lies close to the bones stays (`PosedShoulder` 2 voxels at the shoulder, `PosedHand` a quarter at the hand). `head: face` (the human race): the face-and-hair rule of the standard humanoid's head for a body that is not made one; the head is the torso above where the arms begin. XPiratez's Doom soldier had its face's yellow and pink along the sides of its helmet. `trunk: round` (the Gilldog): its trunk and head as broad as they are tall; drawn from the front crouching, its legs out beside it, it was as broad as that drawing, a barrel across the whole tile. Checked on the player's save, each body rebuilt before and after: of its 37 different bodies 25 change, the 16 Deep Ones and the 9 humans (police, civilians), by 24 to 746 cubes of doubled arm; the Gilldog; the Janissaries, the squad's suits, dogs and rats are cube for cube the same. In the game on that save, by day, before (the three entries taken out of the rules) and after: the police officer's second forearm at the hip, the Deep One's spare forearms and the Gilldog's barrel are gone. TFTD's own Deep Ones: next to no change. Units not named: 280 test bodies cube for cube as before.
- **Still water mirrors** (a player, GitHub issue #213, of the sea's mirror: "it could be expanded to additional surfaces, namely beach sweep in piratez ... DO NOTE the tiles which partially merge with sand", and six saves: "especially if you can make it green"; the owner: "check also #213"). The sea's rule (`water:`) draws a whole tile as waves and carries it past the map's edge: no good for a swamp, whose terrain has dozens of floors that are water and bank, water and lilies, water and reeds. A second table, `stillWater:`, names terrain data sets and the palette's colour groups (0 to 15, a colour's number over 16) that are water on their floors; `records:` narrows it to some records. A floor of such a set on which 15 in 100 of the art's pixels or more are of those groups is still water (`VoxelModel::stillWater`): with *Reflections*, on the GPU renderer, the pixels of its top that are of those groups mirror as the sea does (one more ray, the sky or what it meets), tilted by ripples a third of the sea's waves (`PondSwell`), and keep their own colours under the mirror; every other pixel of the tile is as drawn. Nothing goes on past the map's edge. And where the game gives such a floor only a few voxels (the deep water of the big mods' swamps and beaches: 5 of a tile's 256 columns, so far filled in as a ghost seen through), the rest of its art is filled in solid, whatever *Parts without shape* says: it is the water's face. Named: `FORESTSWAMP`, `FORESTSWAMP_SNOW`, `TUNDRASWAMP`, `BEACH` (blue), `FORESTSWAMP_WASTE`, `NUKE_WATER_2` (green), `IDT_FARM_WATER` and `MOUNTMUD` (slate blue), for XPiratez and The X-Com Files alike. Checked on the player's six saves by day: the swamp, the frozen swamp, the waste's and the nuclear swamp (green), the rice farm's paddies mirror trees and sky; the mountain's puddles, a few tiles, change by 13 in 255 a pixel seen from above; the sea of a TFTD island as before; the CPU renderer draws the swamp whole, without the mirror. Not done of what the report names: city fountains and XPiratez's sewers (a liquid drawn as a box: another kind of surface), and a dry river bed (`SAVANNABEACH`: no water in its art).
- **A weapon's underside can be named** (a player, GitHub issue #220: The X-Com Files' "p90 upside down in first person ... it IS correct in voxel mode"). The weapon in hand takes for a gun's underside the side of its inventory picture that stands out further from the picture's middle line: the grip, the magazine. The P90's sight stands out 7.9 pixels and its grip 7.1, so it was held belly up, its magazine underneath. The mod's item table has a new word, `underside: left | right | top | bottom` (the side of the picture, as drawn, that is the underside), and an entry for the P90 (`right`). Looked for a general rule first, on the inventory pictures of all 861 hand-held firearms of UFO, The X-Com Files and XPiratez that stand up in their pictures: the underside as the side of the bore's line with more of the picture disagrees with the present rule on 236 of them and is the worse of the two (a rifle's stock lies off its muzzle's line); taking the picture's right side on a near tie would turn 149 over, most of them rightly as they are. So the measure stays, and a gun it gets wrong is named. Checked in a battle: the P90 in hand with its green magazine on top.
- *Voxel units*: **the arm rule reaches the unit it was reported on** (GitHub issue #219 again; the owner: "check also ... #219"). The player's picture shows a civilian of The X-Com Files (white vest, red trousers, the vest's white on his arms), no soldier: the rule above, made for the people the table names `humanoid: standard`, never reached him.
