The *Hitboxes* outline and the soldier's own lines with *Full sprite hitboxes* follow the lying card. The big creatures stand upright as before: the walkers (the Sectopod, the Reaper, TFTD's Triscene) and TFTD's Hallucinoid and Xarquid. No setting.
- *Voxel units*, a quality pass on the bodies (the owner's reports on pictures of a Sectoid and a Muton):
  - Bodies twice as fine on a desktop (no setting: the one switch is *Voxel units*, now on the first Settings page; a headset keeps the coarser bodies).
  - Slimmer limbs: legs standing side by side are no deeper than one and a half times their width (a Sectoid's legs seen from between two directions were twice as thick as drawn), and each leg or arm of the front drawing is paired with one of the side drawing (no doubled legs from the side).
  - A direction's picture paints only the voxels that face it; the far side keeps the picture of the direction that faces it (an eye no longer runs through the head as a dark band).
  - Nothing floats: of a body only the biggest connected piece is kept, in every view; a hovering unit (a Cyberdisc, a tank) is its solid body, without the glow drawn under it.
  - Held items are the item's own model, made from its inventory picture as the weapon in your hands is, whole from every side (it was repainted and trimmed by each direction's few weapon pixels: ragged ends).
  - No slits, ever: each voxel is drawn as a little cube (its six-sided outline, where it was a square facing the eye, smaller than the gaps on a diagonal surface), every voxel on one grid (the held item and the views' pieces too), and each view's shown shape has a closed skin. The owner: "Voxels are always mini cubes and pack perfectly!"
  - A view's added pieces (what one direction's drawing has and the body lacks: a toe, a claw's tip) stay only within a voxel of the body; farther ones read as a limb too many from a little beside their direction.
  - A code review's fixes: on fine bodies the walk bobbed half as much and the pieces' depth search stopped short; a one-frame walker's pieces over its legs swing with them; a fall's painting leaves the items their colours.
  - A corpse made faint (*See-through corpses*) stays its 3D body, faint; it turned into the flat corpse picture.
  - Deaths collapse as the sprites do, with volume: each death frame's own picture is given thickness and shown in turn (arms thrown out, knees giving), and the body then lies as its corpse's inventory picture laid on the floor with thickness (the owner: "deaths should not be like planks"; the standing body was tipped over stiff). A Zombie's Chryssalid rises upright.
  - Effects on a body are particles, and dramatic: tongues of flame all over a burning unit with sparks and thick smoke (the game's flame pictures only with *Particles* off), a burst of bubbles with each breath under the sea, a hard rain of white, yellow and pale blue sparks with flashes on the ground and blown dust under a hovering big unit (its body leaves out the glow its pictures have there).
  - *Full sprite hitboxes* and *Voxel units* say in their help lines what a shot hits: the original shapes, or the whole body.
- *Coloured lights*: a rocket in flight lights the walls and the ground round it in its flame's orange, as the setting's description says (a player: the rocket launcher "should also make a light trail on GPU", as an incendiary round or a plasma bolt does). Rockets and every other explosive round were left out with the plain bullets (`fillFrame`'s `addShot`: the smoke trail's kind was skipped). A thrown grenade whose damage is fire (a mod's Molotov cocktail, an incendiary grenade) lights the same way as it flies, thrown the game's way or as a free grenade of the shooter rules (the owner: "Molotov yes", and all that burns in flight; a burning arrow is a round of fire and lit already). And it sheds embers on its way with either renderer, as a round of fire does (`burnsInFlight`, `spawnTrailSegment`, `drawFreeGrenade`).
- **The 3D VIEW tab redesigned** (GitHub issues #164, #167, #168: "we definitely have settings bloat"). Three sub-tabs: **Settings**, a first page of eight rows (Gameplay, Renderer, Graphics detail, Render scale, Show trajectory, Full sprite hitboxes, Mouse sensitivity, Start battles in 3D); **Controls**; and **Advanced**, every setting under its section, the sections and the settings in each by the alphabet. Nothing is removed.
- **Gameplay** replaces *Playstyle*: six presets, each keeping what the one before it set: **FAITHFUL** (soldiers' 3D view on top of OXCE), **Extended+** (the default: the map camera, unit names, hitboxes shown, exact aim), **TACTICUS** (cameras as the eyes see, the accuracy line, the rangefinder, the compass's step costs), **XMbl** (akimbo, lean shots, modern throwing, full sprite hitboxes, damage numbers, sidesteps facing the view, a far clear view), **FullQoL** (every feature on that leaves the rules of turning, moving and shooting alone), **FPS** (free turning and walking, FPS-style shooting, quick deaths, fall damage). Changing one of their 45 settings by hand makes it **Custom**, and the mix is kept (`gameplaySwitches`, `choosePreset`, `presetSwitchChanged`, options `fpsPreset` and `fpsCustomPreset` in place of `fpsPlaystyle`).
- **Graphics detail**: Low, Medium, High, by the *Renderer*. CPU: Low has pixels twice as big and no sun shadows, Medium the HUD's resolution, High the battlescape's. GPU: Low the HUD's resolution, Medium the display's with *Render scale* on Auto, High that with true colour, smooth light, soft shadows, ambient occlusion and the lights' effects. A ray colours one pixel at every level but the CPU's Low. Changing the *Renderer* sets the level again for it (`graphicsSwitches`, `rendererChanged`, option `fpsGraphicsDetail`).
- **New defaults**, those of Extended+ and of issue #167: the map camera Small, hitboxes Always, parts without shape All, every shot's line shown, see-through crops and corpses, camera artifacts Light, futuristic night vision; UFO lights, hit sounds, combat music, the accuracy line and fall damage off; pixel size 1. A profile from before keeps every value it has, and reads Custom where they match no preset.
- The manual's *Renderer* entry said the GPU renderer had run on Windows only under Wine and not yet on a Mac: it has been played on both since.
- *Throwing*'s two values are named Classic and Modern (they were "Classic (tile)" and "Modern (continuous)", too long for the value's column: they took two lines); the help line says which is by tile and which continuous.
- **V** is a second key for the isometric / 3D switch beside F8; the auto shot has no key of its own by default (the wheel and the next fire mode key reach it). A profile from before, where V was the auto shot's, kept it so for a while and had no second key; since the owner's ruling of 2026-11-21 (see above) it too has V on the switch and no key on the auto shot (`settleOptions`). On the Steam Deck's layout the left trackpad's down click sends V: the view switch.

### 1.14 (2026-11-13)

Hot-fixed 2026-11-16 (the first 33 entries, folded into the release):

- New setting *Open doors as ghosts* (Graphics, YES by default: the owner asked for the fix, then for a setting): what is left of a sliding door once it is open (a UFO's door, a mod's electric door) is a ghost, seen through and never in the way (GitHub issue #165: "any voxels without hitbox should become ghost when open, to prevent visibility hampering"). The game has an open door as no obstacle; the last frame's picture still showed part of it, and that stood solid in the doorway (`openDoorModel`, in `snapshotTile` once the sliding is done). NO: solid, as before.
- Through night vision, what is seen through a window stays in the goggles' colours in the full-colour picture too (GitHub issue #166, "gun fire makes night vision flash windows not night vision": with *True colour*, glass mixed its pale blue into the goggles' green, so the view through a window was blue-grey, and it changed with every shot's light). The card's glass tint now keeps to the palette's own answer under the goggles (`uGoggles`, `FirstPersonGpu::Frame::goggles`).
- The Lightning's door opens once (the owner: "as the door would open twice in 3d and skips a bit"). Its record lists four pictures twice (shut, three of opening, shut again, the three again); the map runs through all eight in a blink, but at the 3D view's pace the door was seen to open, shut and open again. A sliding door now plays only as far as each frame is a new picture, at the same pace a frame, and then shows the game's last frame (`snapshotTile`, `slidingFrames`). Doors with eight different frames, the UFOs', slide as before.
- The weapon in hand no longer flashes as you walk (the owner: "when walking in gpu renderer my weapon in first person flashes"). Its shade was the light where the eye is, cut to a whole shade every frame: walking, the eye bobs and moves between tiles, the light hovered at a shade's edge, and the whole weapon went a shade lighter and darker about four times a second (measured on a street at dusk: 26 changes in a five-second walk, on either renderer; now none). The light is followed over a quarter of a second and the shade moves only when it is well into another (`FirstPersonView::getEyeLight`, `_weaponLight`, `_weaponShade` in FirstPersonModeWeaponInHand.cpp).
- With *True colour*, a pixel the weapon in hand or the HUD drew is theirs whatever its colour: where it had the very palette colour of the world behind it, it took the world's full colour instead (`FirstPersonMode::blit`, by the canvas; the card's own overlay in `PresentSource` the same).
- New setting *Sidesteps face the view* (Movement, NO by default; a player, GitHub issue #163: "strafe should always try to maintain looking towards mouse", and for steps back "turn, and strafe left / right: this presents a front / side profile rather than side / rear"). With *Turning costs TU*, every step but the one the view looks along is the game's sidestep, walked with a facing chosen by the view (`sidestepFacingFor`: the view's; an eighth off it for a step half back; side-on for a step straight back), the soldier turned to it first through the game's own turn (`commitStep`; the movement asks for the step again when the turn is done, and held keys carry on through it), its price counted with the step's (`sidestepTurnCost`, in `stepAllowed` and the compass's step costs). `faceLookDirection` leaves a soldier side-on after a step back while the view looks the same way.
- The free cursor comes back where you left it: the game notes where the cursor is when the mouse is captured for looking around, and gives it back there (the first time: in the middle), with one move of the pointer (`Game::updateMouseCapture`).
- On a Mac's Retina display the pointer went to twice the place asked for (the owner's Mac: the cursor jumped to the bottom right of the screen when it was given back; after looking around it showed in one place and jumped to another; and now and then the mouse turned nothing until a click). The fault is in sdl12-compat, the stand-in for SDL 1.2 the app carries: it scaled a pointer's place into the pixels drawn where SDL2 wants the window's points, two pixels to a point on such a display. The Mac app's copy is corrected as it is built (`tools/macos/sdl12-compat-hidpi-warp.patch`, applied by `tools/macos/build_libs.sh`); the game's own drag scrolls and list arrows, which move the pointer too, land right with it. Read from the two libraries' sources and fitting every symptom, but not yet seen working on the Mac. Earlier tries in the game itself (a second and third placing, a check of the first movement, the pointer sent to the middle before a capture) are removed: they moved the pointer through the same fault.
- *Renderer* GPU: the programs for what comes after the rays (the units and effects, the bloom, the screen wash, the frame put together in full colour) are built when the card starts, as the battle loads, not with the first frame of the 3D view (the owner's Mac: the first click on 3D after installing a new version stood still for two seconds; Apple's driver builds each program slowly once and keeps it). The log says how long they took ("the programs after the rays built in ... ms"). What a driver still does at a program's first use is not moved by this.
- With *Turning costs TU*, walking the way you look (W alone) is no longer a sidestep (GitHub issue #161, "movement should act like turn to shoot": looking south with the body still facing south-west, holding W sidestepped south tile after tile, a TU more each, the armour's side to the enemy). The soldier turns to face the step, as the game's walk turns, and walks at the walk's price; A, D and the diagonals with them sidestep as before (`FirstPersonMode::isSidestep`). Measured: three tiles east from a body facing north-east, 12 TU and facing east after (15 TU and still north-east before).
- New setting *Render scale* (SCREEN, 100% by default; 50, 60, 67, 75, 85, 100% or Auto), for *Renderer* GPU on weak cards (the owner, after a tester's "wish it was a little better optimized" on a Steam Deck): the card casts fewer rays than the picture has pixels and magnifies the result on the card (`UpscaleSource`, `FirstPersonGpu::Frame::rayScale`; the units, effects and HUD after it at full resolution, as everything after the rays finds pictures of the view's size). Auto is a dynamic resolution (`FirstPersonView::noteDrawTime`, `RenderScaleSteps`). Measured on an integrated Intel Arc at 1280×800: 14.0 ms a frame at 100%, 10.9 at 75%, 7.9 at 50%. At 100% nothing changes: the same rays, checked against the CPU's as before.
- Tried with it and dropped, by measurement: leaping over runs of empty and low tiles in the rays' walk (a distance field over the tiles). On the same card the walk's steps are a small part of a frame (every ray walked to the map's edge, nothing looked into: 3.3 ms of 14), and the leap's own sums cost what it saved: a town 14.5 ms with and without, a desert 13.1 against 13.2.
- *Light shafts*: the light in the air is bounded (GitHub issue #155, "god lights": a cloud of liquid nitrogen over a space station's floor lamps was a white glare over two thirds of the view). The card added the air's light up along the ray with no limit: every lamp in reach and every step through the smoke counted in full. Now the air at a point is no brighter than fully lit however many lamps reach it, the air nearer the eye dims what the air behind it adds, and all of it together adds at most 45% of white (`lightShafts` in FirstPersonGpuShaders.h, `ShaftMost`). Thin smoke and single lamps look as before.
- A wall one goes through (a door, a curtain) keeps its own look on both faces on a mirrored craft too (GitHub issue #156: a curtain was a curtain on one side only), and a building on a mission site is no craft to mirror: with no UFO in the battle, only the player's own landed craft is mirrored (`findMirrors`: the craft's map data taken from the craft in this battle, not from every craft the mod has; XPiratez builds its temples from a craft's map data). A building's walls came out in patches, the far side's look where the mirror found any and their own elsewhere ("a stair reflection", "a metal tile"); they now show their own design whole, or take *Wall paint* by their rooms. A mission site whose map is a real alien ship is no longer mirrored either.
- *Damage numbers* are the 3D view's top layer: drawn after the enemy markers, the downed icons and the facing rings (GitHub issue #160: a stack of markers over a unit hid its number).
- Drag scroll on the map sets the cursor back to the middle only as it nears the screen's edge, not on every mouse movement, and a movement of nothing does nothing (`BattlescapeState::mapOver`; GitHub issue #158: in fullscreen at a resolution that isn't the display's own, holding the drag scroll's button dropped the frame rate; it didn't in a window). Not reproduced here (Linux, and the Windows build under Wine, in a window): a fix by the code, to be confirmed by the player. The scroll itself is unchanged (it goes by the movements added up).
- A part whose later frames are its first frame's art, pixel for pixel, under another sprite number doesn't animate: all its frames are one model (`getModel`, `_sameArtModels`; GitHub issue #157, "pulsing texture": XPiratez's NUKE_CITY_2 walls took their *Wall paint* in the first frame only, one tick in eight). It also saves the frame-to-frame dissolve on such parts.
- *True colour* works with the game's plain video output (GitHub issue #153: "GPU mode not seeming to make changes", on a Steam Deck and on Windows). The 3D world's full colours can only show on a full-colour display, and the game's plain output opens an 8-bit one: True colour, and with it *Smooth light*, *Coloured lights*, *Reflections* and *Light shafts*, did nothing unless the player had also chosen the game's OpenGL output or a 32-bit scaler, and nothing said so. Now, with *Renderer* GPU, *3D resolution* Display and *True colour* set, the display is opened in full colour (`FirstPerson::wantsFullColorDisplay`, read where `Screen` chooses its depth; the mode resets the display when the three change, `FirstPersonMode::think`), and the game's screen, 32-bit too then, is copied onto it by the add-on (`flipFullColor`: the engine's own magnifying is for an 8-bit screen or a scaler). Measured on a night scene with a flare: 159 colours on screen before, about 29,000 after.
- New setting *Close range: from the eye* (Combat, NO by default; the owner, on GitHub issue #148: "close range forced centre targeting", "force a shot from eyes"): with the game's own targeting, a straight shot at a point within 32 voxels of the eye leaves from the eye's place across the tile, as an FPS-style round does, all else the game's. A flag on the action (`BattleAction::eyeOrigin`, read by `TileEngine::getOriginVoxel` beside `freeAim`), set by `fire` and the shot preview (`closeEyeShot`, `CloseEyeRange`).
- *Jump over low obstacles* reworked to the owner's rule on GitHub issue #124: Jump while walking forwards or sideways (not backwards) into an obstacle up to 15 voxels high (was 14, which is the same obstacles: the game's shapes are in layers of two), for 30 time units in all (was the walk's cost alone, the jump free), charged once the soldier is over. A plain hop on the spot now costs 1 time unit in the soldier's turn (was free). Still a setting, off by default; no climbing. `tryVault`, `jump`, `VaultMaxHeight`, `VaultTimeUnits`, `HopTimeUnits`.
- The shot preview's laser says when the round won't land under the crosshair (GitHub issue #148, "off lasers"; the owner: mark it clearly). With the game's own targeting a round leaves from the soldier's weapon point, not the eye: aiming at something a tile or two away, or past a near edge, the game's line meets something else, or its own aim goes for another part of the target. The laser was right but looked like a fault. Now, when its end is, seen from the eye, more than two voxels off the point under the crosshair (never needing more than 0.04 radians; `LaserAwayVoxels`, `LaserAwayAngle`, `LaserAwayMostAngle`), the laser is drawn dimmed, its dot still bright (`TRAJECTORY_AWAY_LINE`), and a red **OFF** shows left of the crosshair (`_txtAimOff`, `_laserAway`); a shot the game will refuse altogether, already dimmed, shows **BLOCKED** there. Only while the laser shows (*Shot preview* with lines for straight shots). FPS-style shooting fires from the eye and has none of this.
- A wall's hidden face never takes the look of a door or of a wall one walks through (GitHub issue #147: in XPiratez's temple a solid wall wore the red of the curtain opposite on the face the map never shows, a way through where there is none). The temple is mirrored as a craft is (`mirroredColor`: a hidden face takes the look of the shown face across the building's middle), and across the middle hung the curtain. A wall part that is a door, or that the game lets one walk through, is now marked (`VoxelModel::wayThrough`) and lends its look to no other face, on the CPU and on the card (`voxelColorAt`); the face then takes the next row's look, or its own. The same for *Wall paint*: such a wall is no donor and keeps its own (`findWallPaints`).
- Posters show on their walls (GitHub issue #138: XPiratez's pin-ups, COMDECOR record 48 and COMBITS record 7, showed only once the wall they hang on was shot away). Such an object is a flat picture one voxel thick on the tile's west or north edge, inside the two voxels of the wall there, which the rays meet first from either side; the map draws the object over the wall. In the 3D view it now stands two voxels into the tile, clear of the wall (`buildModel`, `GhostWallClearance`, `PosterMinExtent`: an object thinner than a wall on that edge, at least 6 voxels high and wide); the game's own voxels for it stay where they are. A poster without voxels (a ghost on that edge, `buildGhost`) likewise.
- A machine without shape that lights its surroundings is no flame (GitHub issue #150: the consoles of XPiratez's radar room, XB1BR record 41, stood as flat pictures turned to the eye, covering the view up close, and hung through the floor seen from the level below). Every object without voxels that the game makes a light source was drawn as a flame, its art as a sprite; now only one whose art's outline changes from frame to frame (`partKind`; of XPiratez's 64 such objects, 28 change their outline, the fires and pulsing lights, and 36 keep it, the machines with blinking lights). The others are parts without shape as any. And an object without voxels that no one can walk through, its art big (500 drawn pixels and more: a console, a rack of machines), is built as a body from its art, solid (the owner: "make an object from the art"): every voxel of the tile whose pixel the art draws, up to the height the art reaches, in that pixel's colour, so from where the map looks it is the art exactly (`artBody`, `buildArtBody`, ghost kind 7). With *Parts without shape* on Lifts too. Shots and sight go through it, as the game gives it no voxels.
- A wall with a window in it, destroyed, is the game's rubble and nothing more (GitHub issue #149: XPiratez's temple walls shot down stood on as frames round a hole, looking solid where rounds pass). `addBrokenPaneEdges` kept the intact part's voxels round its glass for whatever a part with any glass in it breaks into; it was meant for panes of glass (issue #75). Now only for a pane: at least half the intact part's voxels the game's own glass (glass filled into an opening, a look only, doesn't count).
- Damage numbers are drawn over everything, as the enemy markers are (GitHub issue #151): a big unit hit through a window, a third of it showing, had its number hidden behind the wall, where the point over the middle of its head is. They were tested against the depth of what's drawn (`drawDamageNumbers`). Still only for units the squad sees.
- Hitbox outlines and lines keep their colours under night vision, both kinds of hitbox (GitHub issue #152): they went green with the world. A mark's pixel is noted (`_ownColours` 2; on the card the glow's bit 128 and a bit of the notes) and the wash leaves it (`washBands`, the card's wash).
- The *Renderer* setting's description no longer says "Linux for now" (the GPU renderer is on Linux, Windows and macOS).
- New setting *Smooth fire* (Graphics, YES by default, as 1.14 draws fire): 1.14's "Smoother fire" had no setting, and a player looked for one (GitHub issue #146). NO gives the dotted dissolve from before 1.14 (`fpsSmoothFire`, the `mixColours` of `drawSpriteBlend`).
- A body carried in a hand (GitHub issue #143): someone unconscious is named by their own name on the HUD's hand line, as the game's inventory names them (`itemLabel`; it read as the corpse item's name, so a living captive looked dead), and a body (a corpse, someone unconscious) is drawn 1.4 times an item's size and higher, as over the shoulder (`HeldBodyScale`, `HeldBodyLift`, `drawHeldItem`): it looked too small for a grown body.
- *Smoke opacity* and *Night smoke opacity* recalibrated (GitHub issue #145; the owner: "almost translucent at 5%", "completely opaque at 100%"): 0 to 100% (were 5 to 95), and Original follows them too. 0%: no smoke drawn. 100%: nothing shows through (Modern a solid cloud, Original the map's solid dots, drawn as before). Smoke over smoke now adds up to exactly the setting: each layer takes its share of what the smoke before it left showing (`plot` in FirstPersonViewSprites.cpp, `mixLevel`; the same in the card's `EffectsSource`), where layers used to mix one over the other and came out thinner than the setting. `smokeCapAt` gives the cap in whole see-through steps. The defaults (80%, 45%) so look denser than in 1.14 for Modern, and Original's dots are no longer solid at them. And stacking is kept to the least (the owner: "use stacking as minimally as possible for performance"): a Modern cloud is as opaque in its body as the setting says on its own (how thick the smoke is shows in its frame, as on the map; before, thinner smoke was fainter and needed clouds behind it to build up), so the nearest cloud fills a pixel, and smoke behind a pixel that has its fill is passed over before anything is worked out for it, on the CPU (`drawSprite`, `smokeFill`) and on the card (`EffectsSource`). The same picture on both.
- Tactical flares and glass (GitHub issue #141): a flare flew through every pane and landed just behind it (its throw is aimed at the point under the crosshair, which stops on glass). Now a flare is stopped by what the game's shape has, though the view sees through it: a pane of glass, a glass door, the voxels behind a hole in the art (a fence), and it lands on a floor without shape (a catwalk's ghost). The glass filled into a window's opening, a look only, shatters as the flare goes through (`RayContext::thrown` in FirstPersonViewRayWalk.h, `updateFlares`).
- A floor without shape lying flat that is no lift's pad (XPiratez's pool water: no voxels, no floor) is drawn solid, filling its tile as the map draws it, with *Parts without shape* on Lifts too (GitHub issue #142: half its pixels showed with All, "loose tiles of water"; nothing with Lifts). Ghost kind 6 in `buildModel` (FirstPersonViewTerrain.cpp), `artLiesFlat`. A lift's pad stays see-through.
- New setting *Rangefinder* (HUD: Off, Digital, Analogue; Off by default) and key *Range (hold)* (unbound) (GitHub issue #139). Digital: the tiles from the unit to the point under the crosshair, left of it (`_txtRange`). Analogue: a stadiametric scale as in the PSO-1 scope (`drawRangeScale`, FirstPersonModeShooting.cpp): a line and a dotted curve as high over it at each of five marks as a standing soldier (`RangeSubjectVoxels`, 25 voxels, "1.7m") shows at that many tiles; the marks are the first five of a ladder (2 to 120 tiles) that fit an eighth of the view's height through the lens not zoomed in, and zoomed in the curve grows with the picture (up to 4 times), so it reads true at any magnification.
- New setting *Fall damage* (Combat, YES by default, as before): NO lets a soldier step off ledges with *Falling* and land unhurt; before, the only way to have no fall damage was *Falling* off, which also stopped every step off a ledge (GitHub issue #137). `fallDamage` (FirstPersonModeMovement.cpp).
- *Futuristic night vision* with *See-through corpses*: a body or a pile of items gone faint under the crosshair keeps its own colours over the green world; it went green with it (GitHub issue #136). A faint picture that keeps its colours is mixed over the world already in the night vision's colours and marked as its own (`plot` in FirstPersonViewSprites.cpp, the same in the card's `EffectsSource`).
- The Quest build's preset (set once when build 124 first runs; change anything after): *Renderer* GPU, *VR eye picture* 710×770 with *VR foveation* Light and *VR smoothing*, eyes kept on the card in *True colour* with *Smooth light* and *Coloured lights*; *Supersampling*, *Soft sun shadows*, *Shadows from every light*, *Ambient occlusion*, *Light shafts*, *Reflections* and *Voxel units* off. Chosen from one Quest 3 log of an earlier build, not yet measured on a headset.
- New setting: *VR foveation* (Off / Light / Strong; *Renderer* GPU): the middle of each eye's picture keeps a ray a pixel, and toward the edge one ray colours 2×2 and then 4×4 pixels (about a third, or a fifth, of the rays), so a bigger *VR eye picture* costs less. Not yet tried on a headset.
- VR with *Renderer* GPU (from a first run on a Quest 3): the headset's graphics chip is asked for its high power level while it draws the 3D world (it was held at the lowest, from when it only showed pictures); an eye pair the card hasn't finished is shown a frame later instead of being waited for a quarter of a second and left out (a stutter, or a black frame); eyes kept on the card are in full colour with *True colour*, and *Smooth light*, *Coloured lights*, *Reflections* and *Light shafts* work in them; the log has a session's settings.
- Free movement: walking diagonally no longer stutters (a straight step put the camera back inside its tile on the other axis), and two straight steps round a corner cost what the one diagonal step costs (6 time units on plain ground, not 8), where the game would let the soldier walk that diagonal.
- *Renderer* GPU: the frames with a coloured light, with light in the air, and the screen wash are drawn once where a battle begins, so the driver doesn't stop the game at the first shot or hit.
- New setting: *Renderer* (CPU / GPU): the graphics card casts the 3D world's rays, the same picture as the CPU's (Linux, and an Apple silicon Mac; Windows tried under Wine only; the CPU by itself wherever the card can't). A full 1920×1080 view's rays take 6–9 ms on a GeForce RTX 4060 against 35–38 ms on the CPU.
- New setting: *3D resolution* (HUD / Battle / Display): the 3D world as fine as its HUD, as the battlescape, or as the display itself whatever the battlescape's scale (the view laid into the display under the HUD when the frame is shown).
- New setting: *HUD scale* (Auto, x1 to x4): the 3D HUD's magnification on big screens. With *3D resolution* Display it counts the display's pixels, whatever the battlescape's scale.
- Night vision: the sky, the haze far away, unexplored terrain and what is seen through window glass are as bright in the goggles as they are (they are picked as the goggles show them); before, the haze could glare: everything beyond its start (walls, floor and ceiling from 45% of the view distance on) turned bright and speckled, seen in XPiratez at night with a short view distance (GitHub issue #122). And glass: a dark room seen through a window came out at almost any brightness, an unlit ceiling glaring while the part a soldier's light fell on was darker; now what is brighter stays brighter, and glass lifts a dark room by about one shade of the goggles' sixteen. Both renderers.
- A built-in item the map draws in the unit's hand (`fixedWeaponShow`: a shield) shows in the 3D hands too; one it doesn't (a dog's bite) still leaves them empty (GitHub issue #123).
- The small cameras (a soldier's card, the map camera up to Medium) always have full detail, whatever *Pixel size*; with *Renderer* GPU, *Pixel size* Auto is full detail at any size.
- New settings for a finer picture than the old game's, on the graphics card only (*Renderer* GPU), all off by default: *Supersampling* (x2, x4), *Soft sun shadows*, *Shadows from every light*, *Ambient occlusion*. With them off the card's picture is the CPU's, as before.
- New settings *True colour* and *Smooth light* (*Renderer* GPU, *3D resolution* Display, a full-colour display): the 3D world past the palette's 256 colours, its light, haze, sky and shadows blended where the palette's picture dithers. Off by default.
- New settings *Coloured lights* (shots, explosions, fires and muzzle flashes light their surroundings in their own colour) and *Reflections* (the sea mirrors the sky and what stands in it), both with *True colour*, off by default.
- New setting *Alien turn: look as the soldier faces* (off by default): in the alien turn the view stays within 45 degrees of your soldier's facing (GitHub issue #126).
- New setting *Compass: step costs* (off by default): what a step each way, a level up or down and a turn cost in time units, round the compass (GitHub issue #132).
- New setting *Throwing*: Classic (tile), the default, or Modern (continuous): throws and arcing shots go for the exact point under the crosshair, from a start that slides round the unit, so the curve moves smoothly with the aim and a large unit's arc no longer jumps from side to side (GitHub issue #128).
- A bow is held as a bow: upright, its lower half below the screen's edge, its curve towards the crosshair and the string behind, the arrow leaving from the curve's middle (it was held like a gun, a limb's tip pointing at the target). Worked out from the weapon's name; a mod can say `bow: true` or `false` for an item in the 3D item table.
- GPU renderer: it also runs on OpenGL ES 3.2 (used where a device has no OpenGL 3.3; `OPENXCOM3D_GPU_GLES=1` asks for it on Linux, to test); *Soft sun shadows* casts its inner rays only at a shadow's edge; the tiles' snapshot is kept between frames for tiles that didn't change; a craft's mirror made anew is always sent to the card; at an even HUD scale the aim goes through the crosshair's exact middle.
- *UFO lights and aura*: a building a mod lists among its UFOs (XPiratez's plants, mansions and bunkers) is no longer lit as one: a UFO has a power source, a navigation console or a grav lift, a building has none (the UFO's coloured light was dithered over and inside such buildings).
- A battle starts a little quicker: the map of the crafts' mirrored sides is made once, not once for each of the view's cameras.
- *Damage numbers*: a hit on an energy shield (X-Com Files, XPiratez) shows what the shield lost, in white, before the health, armour and stun; a hit the shield took whole no longer shows a 0 (GitHub issue #133).
- An enemy's shot knocked aside by your soldier's close-quarters check no longer throws that soldier's view round; the view follows a knocked-aside shot only for the unit that fired it (GitHub issue #125).
- Walking into a unit you don't see (behind a curtain you can walk through but not see through) stops at the tile's edge as at a wall, where the view lunged forward and snapped back for as long as the key was held (GitHub issue #129).
- New setting *Light shafts* (with *True colour*, off by default): the flashlight, flares, lamps and coloured lights show as beams and glows in smoke and under the sea.
- Faster on the graphics card: *Ambient occlusion* costs under half of what it did (most of its look-ups were in open air), *Supersampling* no longer casts its extra rays on texture seen close up (only at edges, fine far texture and strong lines), and the glow round lights is spread over the processor's threads.

### 1.12 (2026-11-11)

- New: *Unseen terrain* (Mist / Fog / Visible) and *View distance* (60 to 200 tiles).
- New: *Fog of war in 3D* (Off by default): the floor below the stairs shows as you look at it (#115).
- New: *Wall paint* (Each side by default): a wall's face the map never shows takes the paint of the side it faces, a house's outside from behind, not its rooms' paint (#111).
- New: *Enemy markers* (Clear shot by default): the markers over the enemies in the colours of the map's buttons: red seen, green seen by the squad only, yellow in a clear line of fire but hidden (smoke, dark, camouflage); As map; All red as before (#117).
- New: *Wounded markers* (On by default): our own wounded and those about to pass out get markers too, in their buttons' blue and purple and numbered as the buttons; a body's over where it lies, none over the soldier you look through (#117).
- New: *Damage numbers* (HUD, off by default): a hit's damage rises over the unit in 3D and on the map: health red, armour blue, stun purple, a grey 0 when armour stopped it.
- New: *Ambient sounds* (On by default): rain and wind in the weather that has them, muffled under a roof, and the fires near you crackling from their side, made by the game; Quiet; Off.
- New: with *Full sprite hitboxes*, *Hitboxes* outlines what the shots really test, in the colour of the unit's side (a unit drawn as a *Voxel units* body: not yet).
- New: *Camera artifacts* (Normal by default): the helmet cameras' static and glitches as they are; Light, lighter, no glitch over 0.15 s; None, a clean feed. A fallen soldier's camera keeps its static at every setting (#121).
- Fixed: *Pixel size* goes round (after 3 back to Auto).
- Fixed: with *Full sprite hitboxes* a unit is hit exactly as drawn: not two voxels lower, its picture's parts beyond its own tile too (bats' wings, a Cyberdisc's rim), thin parts at every pixel, and up close the hit shape leans with the picture.
- Fixed: with *Full sprite hitboxes* a walking or flying unit is hit where the 3D view draws it between two tiles, not half a step ahead or behind.
- Fixed: with *Full sprite hitboxes* arcing shots hit the picture they come down on (XPiratez's bows at a Shambler went for the floor beside it, the curve drawn straight up off the screen): a big unit is aimed at its middle (#119).
- Fixed: in 3D a guided missile took any number of waypoints (F8 back and forth); now the launcher's cap as on the map, and 3D counts them (WAYPOINTS 3/9).
- Fixed: a built-in weapon used from an empty hand counts as the built-in one when both hands are full (#110).
- Fixed: a unit straight below or above no longer spins or twitches as you shoot it: drawn steadily, from the map's side (#112).
- Fixed: with turning by the rules, a sidestep with too few time units no longer jitters: it costs what the game charges, its time unit more than a walk (#114).
- Fixed: *Downed unit icons* only for the body the crosshair is on (the nearest picture: a standing unit there shows its name only), and on Always they hang lower, off a unit behind the body (#116).
- Fixed: our own wounded or stunned soldiers in view no longer get a red enemy marker over them (#117).
- Fixed: a sprint walks on past enemies coming into sight, as en passant does, in every game; any walk in 3D stops when the soldier is shot at, as on the map (#118).
- Fixed: a round that lands before a frame is drawn (a laser up close) starts at the gun's muzzle, with its flash and kick, not off the line of sight (#120).
- Fixed: setting values that fit their column (seven renamed: *Wall paint*'s Inside and out and Every room to Each side and All rooms, *Enemy markers*' Line of fire and As the map to Clear shot and As map, *Fog of war in 3D*'s As the map to As map, *Stat bars*' Percentage to Percent, *Sprite upscaling*'s Smooth + detail to Smooth+).

### 1.11 (2026-11-04)

- **Skies by the weather and the place** (a player, GitHub issue #94: "Slightly disorienting to have a Blue or stary sky, and rain"; the owner: "check all special missions so sky is ok!"): rain and snow fall from overcast, a grey sky (paler for snow, darker at night) under a drifting deck of cloud, with no sun, moon or stars and no sun shadows, every face lit from above. A battle's sky goes by the mod's new `skies:` table (by mission type, else a terrain of the map's blocks, else the environment the big mods give a battle). Space and orbit are black with stars and the sun, nothing past the map's edge and space under it; from XPiratez's orbital stations the Earth lies below, its clouds drifting and its air glowing over its edge; over the Moon the Earth's disc hangs in the black sky, lit on the sun's side; Mars has a butterscotch sky by day with a smaller sun, stars by night and no clouds (Cydonia, the big mods' Mars); XPiratez's Shadowlands, dreams and Doom lie in a violet void. The weather goes by the environment too (acid rain, the dust storm), and there is none in space or under the sea. Round two (the owner: "check all special missions in xpiratez, tftd, ufo and xcom files. Correct as necessary"; 518 New Battles): the big mods' copy of TFTD's sea is water, so the sea runs on past their islands, ports and ships, at the deck's height round the liners and cargo ships; TFTD's own liner and cargo ship float on their sea past the hull; and 19 more maps lie under rock (XPiratez's Thule base, Old Earth metro and sewers, its ninja bases' underground stages, the City of the Dead's sewers, the Rabbit Crypt and the Realm of Fear; X-Com Files' Cyberweb lair, smugglers' refuge, brainsucked lair and ooze nest).

- **Reload from any clip carried** (the owner, GitHub issue #95: "reloading should use compatible spare clips from anywhere in the soldier's inventory"): the game's reload always looked through the whole inventory (belt, backpack, pockets, shoulders, the other hand) and took the clip cheapest to reach. With the big mods' extended reload cost (XPiratez, X-Com Files) the clip's move to the hand is charged too, and when that was more than the soldier had left, R did nothing, silently. In 3D, R now says why when nothing loads: "Already loaded", "No spare clip for this weapon" or "Not enough time units to reload (23 needed)". With Ctrl, Alt or Shift held, R is the game's.

- **The flight switch kept** (the owner, GitHub issue #96): a soldier's flight switched on or off with **B** is kept in the save (`flightSwitch`, beside `mapAkimbo`). Before, after a load every flyer flew only if it was in the air, so one walking on with flight on had it off again.

- **A door under the crosshair** (the owner, GitHub issue #97; then: "Move it to the left of the crosshair"): a little green door left of the crosshair when the wall its line meets first is a door you have seen, a swinging door or a UFO's; on your turn, and not while a unit is under the crosshair (the line can pass over a small alien's head onto a door behind it). Under TFTD's sea it's teal: its palette has no true green.

- **T'leth's ceiling** (the owner, after a campaign run of the final mission: "How does ceiling look like in final? Ok?"): T'leth's first stage and the artifact site's second showed their rock black overhead, under night vision and the flashlight too: the commonest colour of the walls round the map's edge is black at every shade in the deep sea's palette. A colour too dark to show its hue is passed over for the next commonest, the map's own (a dark green).

- **Smoke drawn for less** (a player, GitHub issue #92; the owner: "i would still draw all smoke but check if we can do it efficiently ... and check if it hogs down the performance"): every puff is still drawn, but a puff off the view or wholly behind what's already drawn is left out, and each draws only its own rows and the runs of its coloured pixels. A grenade's cloud of 94 tiles at 1920×1080: 1.6 ms a frame with Modern smoke ahead (2.0 before), 2.75 from inside it (3.2), 5.5 with Original (7.1); on one core at 640×400 1.9, 3.1 and 10.5 ms (2.5, 4.3 and 12). The picture is the same to the pixel, checked frame by frame.

- **The game's line under the top bar** (a player: "When the game triggers Bug Hunt mode, the text layers over the buttons along the top bar that the mod adds. If it's possible to move the bug hunt text down a line when it spawns, that would take care of the issue I think."): OXCE's line at the top left of the screen (its debug messages, and "Bug Hunt mode activated: enemies are visible on the minimap!" each turn once bug hunt mode is on) starts one line down, under our top bar, on the map and in 3D; right of OXCE's touch buttons when they show, and under the map camera's window while that's open (the window starts where the line would be).

- **The enemy buttons at the centre right** (the owner, with a player's picture: "the icons for spotted enemies (green and red squares) are a bit in the middle in 3d - should we position them to the center right of the screen - independent of resolution?", then: "center right - still space for red arrows of enemies"): in full 3D the game's buttons for the enemies in sight stood where the classic HUD has them, off towards the middle of a wide screen. Now they stand in a column at the right, under the compass's middle, those showing centred on the view's height, at the HUD's scale on every screen size, and clear of the red markers at the view's right edge; on a low screen (the Original battlescape scale) they start under the compass's lines. Zoomed in, SCOPING ends left of them. The classic HUD keeps them in its own place.

- **The logo for the downloads** (the owner: "Use logo for icons in release targets, bundle the logo image in releases also"): the AppImage's icon and that of the menu entry the archive's installer makes are the logo at 256 pixels (128 before); the Windows zip has the logo's icon for a shortcut to `play.bat` (`openxcom3d.ico`; `openxcom.exe` has carried it since 1.09); and every download has the logo's picture (`openxcom3d_logo.png`).

- **Flight across the air with Falling off** (a player, GitHub issue #99: "Turning "falling" off when midair suddedly disabled horrizontal flight, mid air."): with *Falling* off, a soldier flying (flight on) steps sideways through the air again, as on the map; the rule that keeps walkers from stepping off a ledge with *Falling* off caught flyers too, so in the air, where no tile around has a floor, every step was refused (since 0.8.2).

- **Landing keeps flight on** (a player, GitHub issue #100: "Rather than touching down instantly disabling flight, make it do it when page down is pressed when already on the ground"): a soldier taken up into the air without the flight switch (the map's own up button or path, or a save from before 1.11) has it switched on in 3D, as if by **B**, so W into the ground or a roof no longer ends the flight: Page Down on the ground (or B) does. Before, such a soldier flew only while in the air, and the first touch of a floor switched flight off.

- **Live motion scanner, a setting** (a player, GitHub issue #101: "Motion scanner stays up when moving ... can very much break things ... but also potentially neat feature?"; the owner: "live 3D motion scanner - on (leave as is), off (mimic vanilla as possible, but keep the correct orientation of screen and live screen updating"): the scanner held up in 3D stayed up while the soldier walked and through the aliens' turn, a live radar for one use. Now that's the setting *Live motion scanner*, off by default: one look from where it was used, as the game's own scanner (a step to another tile or the end of the turn puts it away, using it again costs again), still turning with the view and sweeping. In the *Playstyle*: Faithful off, FPS on (a profile set to FPS before gets it on).

- **Smoke by the soldier's own eyes** (a player, GitHub issue #98: "if a unit is visible, let me see it, if a unit is invisible, don't let me see it"): an enemy only squadmates see (its button green) isn't drawn when smoke lies between it and the soldier looked through, only its marker, as for one behind a wall; before, every enemy the squad saw was drawn plainly through smoke. Enemies the soldier sees stay clear of the smoke, as before. And smoke is drawn as the map draws it again, as in 0.8 (the owner: "the simplest 0.8"): no wall where the soldier's sight runs out (the grey bands and shapes in the player's pictures), no stacked layers; Modern's clouds see-through up to *Smoke opacity*, now a maximum (smoke over smoke builds up to it and no further; 80% by day and 45% at night by default, up to 95%), Original's dots solid and still, exactly as on the map. Cheaper too: the rays no longer trace smoke, and Original no longer stacks.

- **A tank's turret switch** (a player's report, XPiratez: "some tanks have turrets! ... maybe for a tank - turret only button ... when button is pressed the view only moves the turret and spends only turret turning TUs"; the owner: on the top bar, off at first, kept with the save): the **TURRET** button on the top bar for a unit with a turret, and a key (none at first, set one under Controls). While it's pressed, turning the view turns only the turret, by the game's own turret turn (as the map's Ctrl turn and its aiming: a time unit an eighth with *Turning costs TU*), the hull staying as it is; off, the whole tank turns at its full turning cost, as before.

- **A melee blow's hit chance** (a player, GitHub issue #106: "Unlike guns, no accuracy is stated"): the crosshair's shot info shows a blow's hit chance as it shows a shot's, **MELEE 23% 4TU**: the game's own, as the map's action menu gives it for the blow (the soldier's melee accuracy with the weapon), green, in either targeting style.

- **Ctrl+End in 3D** (a player, GitHub issue #104: "ctrl end 'debug full bright' doesn't work in 3d"): OXCE's debug light, which Ctrl+End steps through, lit only the map. The 3D view now follows it as the map does: the light halved (and the units no darker than night vision leaves them), then everything at full light, then back; the sky keeps the time of day, as the map has none.

- **Sprite upscaling, finer** (the owner, after AI upscalers were tried against our xBRZ and set aside: "Keep xBRZ! Can we tune xBRZ we have to produce better results for us", then: "add S4 and S6 to the main!"): xBRZ stays, the steps around it are new. Every upscaled pixel takes a shade of one of the colour ramps the original has right there, so an edge between two colours no longer picks up a stray third colour (13.8% of the pixels before, none now) and armour recolours and the view's shading work on it as on the original; the steps of the shading inside a ramp are softened (never across an outline or into another colour), so faces and muscles lose the blocks of the original pixels; *Smooth + detail*'s sharpening counts only the same colour, without the dark and bright rims and dots it left at edges. A fourth choice, **Finest**, the new default (a profile saved before keeps its choice): six times bigger instead of four, the finest up close, with one-pixel eyes and highlights kept at their shade; its frames take 2.25 times the memory (about 60 MB for the 400 kept) and about 4 ms each to make, once, on the upscaling thread. The same frame always comes out the same: no flicker in walks, deaths, explosions or smoke. (AI upscalers made up detail that changed from frame to frame, with the drawing's style.)

- **One big blast for a chain of explosions** (a player, GitHub issue #105: "Currently, shooting something like a barrel pile always makes 2 explosions, instead of 1 big boom"; the owner: "It should be one big blast but explosions shown at each explosive and short delay between them to really emphasize the chain drama"): with *Instant chain explosions*, the first barrel a shot set off played its own blast, and only when it was over did the rest settle, with a sound of their own. Now the blast that sets a chain off (a grenade's, a rocket's, or that first barrel's) plays as ever, with the big explosion's sound, and the whole chain settles in that same moment, the game's own outcome; a fireball goes up at each explosive in turn, in the order the game sets them off, 120 ms apart (closer in a long chain, the whole ripple within a second and a half), each tile's wreckage and fire showing as the fireball nearest to it grows over it, and the dead fall once the ripple has passed. An explosion that sets nothing off plays exactly as before.

- **Melee at a body on a diagonal tile** (a player, GitHub issue #107: "Melee striking down bodies doesn't work diagonally"): with *Melee hits walls and downed*, a blow at a body lying on the floor struck only one in the tile the soldier faced, so with the crosshair on a body diagonally beside them (the head looks 45 degrees round with *Turning costs TU*) nothing was struck. Now the body under the crosshair takes the blow, at the soldier's feet or on any tile next to them, diagonally too, as a standing unit does: the soldier turns to it first (an eighth's turn, shown on the compass as HIT +1 TU), and one farther off is out of range. With no body under the crosshair, the one in front or at the feet, as before.

### 1.10 (2026-11-03)

- **Glass only in windows** (the owner: "barn windows in farm are just openings as before without glass - check!", then "railings open"): a wall the game sees through gets glass in 3D only where it's a window. One hole with no bars across it is glass only where the game's own shape stands across most of it (X-Com Files' kiosk glass, single-pane glass doors: sight passes, shots stop); one the game leaves open is an opening, as the map and 1.09 show it: UFO's barn windows, Cydonia's and the alien bases' openings, TFTD's port frames and portholes, X-Com Files' village barns and blasted breaches (70 walls across UFO, TFTD and X-Com Files). A wall whose shape covers under a third of its plane (barbed wire, an arch's thin frame) and the gaps of a railing (its wall stopping short of the top: TFTD's cargo deck) get none; painted glass only on a thin wall (not X-Com Files' ice-cave rock), and no glass fill in a thick one (TFTD's airliner fuselage). Windows with bars, glass doors and painted panels keep their glass.

- **Glass shatters** (the owner: "In 1.09 you just shot through window openings as nothing. Now that we have glass - lets implement that you shoot theough window opening as before - but side effect is that glass shatters (by the travelling bullets). This does not influence any game mechanics - only visual and for visceral feel"): the glass the 3D view fills into a window's opening (where the game's shape is open, so rounds fly through) breaks when a round, a pellet or a thrown item goes through it, or a blast that harms terrain reaches the window (smoke and stun gas don't). Its pale shards fall, those where a round went through flying on with it, a few glinting, and a short crack and tinkle is heard (made in code like the hit ticks, with *Hit, kill and swing sounds* on). The opening stays empty for the rest of the battle, as 1.09 showed it: kept in the save (`openxcom3dBrokenGlass`, which the game itself never reads), in either view (a round flying while the map shows breaks it too). A pane of glass the game destroys itself (a blast tearing out a shop's glass wall; the owner: "explosions can wipe out whole walls with windows also") throws the same shards and sound, beside its debris, and takes its glass with it. Only the look: rounds, sight and the rules go as the game has them.

- **A wall with a small window stays a wall** (the owner, comparing with 1.09: "that should be a solid wall with a window like our previous release"): a pane of glass with holes in its art is glass only where its window is (in each column of the art, from the top to the bottom of the window's panes, with what's drawn among them), the art round it solid. Since the glass of GitHub issue #75 the whole piece of such a wall (the UFO terror map's shop, its wall pieces with a small window each) was glass, see-through from the floor up; now it's the wall and window of 1.09, with glass in the window.

- **Painted glass** (the owner, of X-Com Files' market: "the building should have translucent windows all around"): a wall the game sees through (the map shows the room behind it) whose shape is a solid wall and whose art is mostly pale bluish glass with no see-through pixels, X-Com Files' standard glass panels and glass doors (its markets, offices, labs, prisons, bases), shows what's behind it as a pane of glass, its frame solid, where it showed an opaque panel. The game's shots stop on it, and so does the aim; units behind it stay hidden as on the map, which spots no one through it.

- **Windows of small panes are glass too** (the owner, after GitHub issue #75: "glass by the game's rules"): a wall the game lets sight through, its art a window split by bars into small panes (a house's window in UFO, TFTD's port, X-Com Files' towns), shows a pane of glass where it showed an open hole; and every pane's openings in the game's shape are glass, so a pane is even, not striped. Where the game's shape is open (its rounds and sight go through), that glass is a look only: the aim goes through it to what's behind. Walls the game doesn't see through (the shops' beige walls, white panels and glass doors in X-Com Files' towns) stay solid, as on the map. A glass door swinging open shows its panes again (they had swung as a grey slab since #75).

- **A mod's mana on the 3D panel** (a player, GitHub issue #77: XPiratez's freshness, X-Com Files' sanity): where the map shows the mana bar, the 3D info panel shows it too, a thin bar under the stats as on the map (the soldier card left as it was).

- **Cameras see as the eyes** (a player, GitHub issue #90: an X-Com Files X-Bat's heat vision saw through smoke in the 3D view, its camera saw none of it): a setting (View and camera, NO by default: a realistic camera, as before); YES, the helmet cameras (the soldier card's, the soldier cam, the map camera, a fallen soldier's last picture) see through smoke with the soldier's heat vision, as the 3D view does.

- **Arcing shots and throws at the open sky fly on** (a player, GitHub issues #72 and #91: aimed above the horizon, a grenade launcher's round went for the corner of a tile of air and burst in the open air): the arc goes through the point of air on the crosshair's line and comes down wherever it falls, bursting there; a throw at the sky lands where that arc comes down, within the soldier's reach (the game refused it before). A forced arc that leaves the map is no longer taken as a hit at the edge.

- **Units' shadows from flares** (a player, GitHub issue #73): with *Shadows* on, a unit lit by a tactical flare throws a soft shadow along the ground away from it, longer the lower the flare (up to three tiles), widening and fading towards its end. And the soft shadow under every unit now shows from afar too (it lay inside the floor, where the floor seen a few tiles off missed it).

- **A marker for a strike that did no harm** (a player, GitHub issue #82): a round or blow that struck an enemy in sight but took neither health nor stun (armour, a zero roll, full resistance) marks the crosshair with a little blue shield to its lower left, with a duller tick (under *Hit, kill and swing sounds*).

- **The fire modes from quantity to quality** (a player, GitHub issue #71: "Aimed in the middle is confusing"): the wheel and J go auto, snap, aimed (then melee, throw, prime as before); a gun still starts on snap, so aimed is one notch down and auto one up.

- **Akimbo 20% less accurate, not 30%** (a player, GitHub issue #87: the penalty stacks with the game's own for a two-handed gun, which made light two-handed guns unmanageable): each of two guns fired together keeps 80% of its accuracy (FPS style: the spread a quarter wider, not 1.4 times), in 3D and on the map.

- **Futuristic night vision** (a player, GitHub issue #88): a Graphics setting, NO by default (realistic, all green, as before); YES leaves units, bodies and items their own colours under night vision, as the isometric map does.

- **Screen shake and Recoil settings** (a player, GitHub issue #67: for players whom a moving view bothers): *Screen shake* (Graphics) off, nothing shakes the view; *Recoil* (Combat) off, shots don't kick the view up or climb it in a burst (the gun in hand still kicks). Both YES as before.

- **Akimbo in 3D marks the map, and reaction shots fire both guns** (a player, GitHub issue #79): **Y** in 3D also marks the soldier's akimbo on the map (in turn, or both at once with *Akimbo volley*), or clears it, so the map's shots follow; a soldier marked fires both guns on reaction shots too, in either view.

- **A key waiting to be rebound shows it** (a player, GitHub issue #83: with no sign it was waiting, players thought keys couldn't be rebound): on the 3D VIEW tab's Controls a clicked key's row turns gold and a _ blinks in place of its key until a key is pressed; Esc cancels and leaves the key as it was (it closed the options before).

- **The 3D view's own turn and look keys** (a player, GitHub issue #81: someone scrolling the map with W, A, S, D found 3D walking and turning at once): turning and looking up and down in 3D have their own keys (*Turn left*, *Turn right*, *Look up*, *Look down*, the arrows as before) instead of the map's scroll keys, so the map can scroll on W, A, S, D (set in the game's own Controls) while 3D walks on them.

- **No dip when the view goes to another soldier** (a player, GitHub issue #86: in the aliens' turn every camera swap lowered and raised the gun, "as if they were just equipped"): the gun in hand dips out of sight and back only when the same soldier takes another weapon or reloads; looking through another soldier shows his gun at once.

- **Arcing shots and throws at a wall you stand against** (a player, GitHub issue #84: X-Com Files' position markers, fired in an arc a tile off, unfolded behind the soldier or through walls in 3D, never on the map): aimed at the wall at the edge of the soldier's own tile, a throw or an arcing shot aims at the tile across it, as the map is clicked beyond the wall, and the game arcs over it or refuses (they aimed at the soldier's own tile, and what they set down went to the nearest free tile). *Force arcing shots* never forces a round that sets a unit down. And a crash fixed in the game itself: an arc blocked at its very first step (from against a wall, now and then only, by the shot's random deviation) left an empty path, which the game read from its end.

- **Zombies hatch with quick deaths** (a player, GitHub issue #80: "Animation where zombie hatches into chysallid is entirely skipped"): with *Quick deaths* on, a unit that turns into another as it dies (a Zombie, whose death is the Chryssalid bursting out of it) dies the game's own way: its death plays, and the Chryssalid comes once it has (down at once, it hatched at once, unseen).

- **Glass you can see through** (a player, GitHub issue #75, with an X-Com Files save: a shop's glass walls opaque, broken glass leaving nothing; the owner: "just a smooth seethrough pane (like we have for ghost) and when broken only voxels on the edges shown"): a wall the game lets sight through whose art is a frame round a big see-through area is a pane of glass: what lies behind shows through it, a little paler and bluer, smooth, its frame solid; broken, a jagged band of glass stays round the inside of its frame and the middle is open. Every aim stops on glass (the owner): the crosshair's point is on the pane, so a shot goes for it and a blow strikes it (and breaks it). X-Com Files' mission sites set on the map with a UFO's rules (a museum, a bank, a barn, labs) no longer count their town and field parts as UFO hulls, which had kept the shop's glass opaque.

- **The version in the main menu** (a player, GitHub issue #69: without the AppImage's name, no telling the version; the owner: "a smaller space and our version number besides OpenXcom3D", then "Make the version no. Smaller"): the mod's version in small letters just right of the title's big "OpenXcom3D", its foot on the big letters' foot (the owner: "move the version number just a couple of pixels lower"), read from the mod's own metadata, so every bump shows.

- **Auto-equip guns first: the hardest-hitting first** (a player, GitHub issue #76: X-Com Files' soldiers got staffs and swords from our build with the setting off, bows and old SMGs with it on, cannons and gauss from their stock OXCE): OXCE's auto-equip has no ranking; it hands out the craft's kit in the order the items sit in memory (a map keyed by the rules' addresses), which differs between builds and between runs of one build (one battle, run three times on ours: a rifle, a musket, a rifle; on stock OXCE from the same source three rifles). The setting now offers the guns by the power of one round of the strongest ammo for them in the craft, direct fire before launchers (the owner), in a fixed order: in X-Com Files a sonic cannon, a recoilless gun, a heavy plasma, a sonic shotgun, a sonic blasta rifle; in UFO the heavy plasma, the heavy laser, the plasma rifle ... the rifle, then the blaster, rocket and small launchers. Off, OXCE's own, unchanged.

- **Hits on downed units** (a player, GitHub issue #74: units already down showed nothing when shot or struck): a knocked-out unit hit flashes as a standing one does, blood (or sparks) spray from its body on the floor, and a downed enemy hit marks the crosshair (the kill mark when the hit kills it); a hit that only adds stun counts now, so a stun blow makes a standing unit flinch, flash and mark the crosshair too. The flash is a new setting, **Hit flash** (YES; the owner: "This flash on hit should be on/off"), and *Muzzle flashes* off still turns every flash off.

### 1.09 (2026-10-30)

Playtest reports on UFOs from inside, on smoke at night, on a walk through a door that never ended, on shots off the crosshair and on melee by the compass, at floors and straight up, on FPS style's lost snap shot, on names by the picture, on tails in the floor, on corpses in the way and on Page Up, settings for new players, holes under the zoom, a clean view and the motion scanner, full sprite hitboxes, quick deaths, and a logo of its own (patches 189–206).

- **A UFO's light inside it, even** (the owner, playing: "The ufo lights and aura put a while screen overlay of dots (daylight mission)... the aura effect should be only on the outside of the ufo to keep screen clean and sharp in the inside"): the UFO's light was mixed in by a dither, a whole pixel of its colour here and there, which inside a UFO, its soft light everywhere, laid a grid of dark dots over the view. Now the UFO itself (its inside and its hull) takes an even tint: tables of each colour with one to twelve twelfths of the light mixed in, each picked as bright as the colour itself, so a lit wall is one tint and its panels keep their detail; greys take all of it and strong colours little (a red lift pad stays red); inside, about a fifth of the way to the light's colour by day and two fifths at night. The pool on the ground around it keeps its sparkle, and the aura's motes and sparks never start on the UFO's own tiles (its rim, an ellipse, cut inside the corners of a boxy UFO). The grav lifts' beams stay.
- **Smoke opacity, by day and at night** (a player: "non full smoke can be blinding for some players. Suggest a setting for smoke opacity (for not full smoke) defaulted at 40% (or what current is). also suggest seperate night smoke setting, at half of current value by default"): settings *Smoke opacity* (45%, the look before) and *Night smoke opacity* (25%) for the smoke you see through, the thinnest smoke's opacity with thicker smoke more as before; the night value on a night mission (lit smoke on a dark night is the blinding kind) and where the smoke hangs in the dark by day, blended in between. Where sight runs out the smoke stays solid.
- **No walking on out of the map through a door** (a player: "a unit of mine walks into a door backwards or sideways and appears to walk infinitely out of bounds. When swapping to iso, they appear to of stopped in last available tile"): through a door (or a diagonal gap) the camera walks on by itself until it is well inside the new tile, a stretch only 6 voxels wide, and one slow frame (a tenth of a second moves it 6 to 13 voxels) could jump right over it: the camera then walked on for ever, the keys doing nothing, while on the map the soldier stood in the new tile. Now it never goes past the new tile.
- **Shots nearer the crosshair** (a player, with screenshots, issue #36: "odd cases of very visually off shots", "when aiming along flat ground, occasionally lasers jut in strange directions"): with the game's own targeting, when no muzzle point's line reaches the point under the crosshair (along a floor, the line from the gun, lower than the eye, grazes the bumps on the way; over a counter it meets its edge), the shot went for the game's own aim: the tile's middle, or another part of the unit. Now first for the nearest point within 4 voxels (a quarter tile; upwards first) whose line meets the same target near the crosshair as the eye sees it; only then the game's own aim. The game's line of fire and accuracy as before.
- **No turn south at your own tile** (the same report: "Firing into a wall you are up against to the west, somehow forces you to look south"): OXCE turned a unit aiming at its own tile (a wall it stands against, the floor at its feet) to face south, and with *Turning costs TU* charged for the turn: its direction to a point straight above or below was atan2 of 0 and -0. Now it keeps its facing, on the isometric map too.
- **Melee by the crosshair, not the compass** (a player, GitHub issue #25: "aiming at north west cabinet strikes the unit to north with melee"): a blow strikes a unit under the crosshair on any tile next to the soldier, diagonally too, the soldier turning to them first (paid with *Turning costs TU*; the compass says HIT +1 TU); a unit under it farther off is out of range; a wall, door or object under it within reach takes the blow (with *Melee hits walls and downed* or the mod's terrain melee; without, the blow strikes nothing). Only with the crosshair on none of these (open floor, the sky, out of reach) does the blow go the way the soldier faces, as on the map.
- **The unit pointed at by its hitbox** (a player, GitHub issue #26: the name showed only while a pixel of the unit's picture was under the crosshair; X-Com Files' small swarmids, moving oddly, made it blink): the unit you point at, for its name, the middle click, a medi-kit's patient and a melee blow, is now the one whose hitbox the game's own line through the crosshair (or the free cursor) meets, as a shot would, which doesn't change as the picture moves; only else the one whose picture is there (a unit far off, a few pixels across).
- **Tails on the floor, not in it** (a player, GitHub issue #31: X-Com Files' Asmodean Cobra "in the ground" in some rotations; "use the same technique as items"): a unit stands on a card at its feet, and what the isometric art draws below its feet (the front half of the floor's diamond: a Snakeman's tail, a long body in some facings) sank into the floor, ending in a straight cut. Now that part lies flat on the floor in front of the unit, as an item's does; for most units it's only a few pixels of the feet. A unit in the air keeps the upright card.
- **See-through corpses** (a player, GitHub issue #35: very tall corpses, XPiratez's boomasaurs and mongorns, "visually block shots", though the game's shots pass through items): a setting, *See-through corpses* (off at first): the corpse or item pointed at is drawn faint, at the ghost opacity, while you aim past it; aimed at the floor it lies on, solid.
- **Page Up flies** (a player, GitHub issue #32: "if I press page up, its very obvious my intention is to fly. Let it toggle flying on, if NOT inside lift"; the owner: and page down off): off a lift or ladder, Page Up with flight off switches flight on and flies up a level in one press (it only said "Flight off"); Page Down with flight on, standing on the ground, switches it off. W looking up, the lift's arrows and the classic HUD's buttons stay as they were.
- **Settings for new players** (a player, GitHub issue #27: "Many settings would probably be better changed as defaults or renamed to be more friendly to new players"; the owner's picks): new installs start with the game's own mechanics where the 3D view adds its own: *Movement* Tiles, *Flight cost overrides rules* NO (flight at the game's cost; it was free), *Jump over low obstacles* NO, *Night vision needs gear* NO; and *Shotgun pellets in flight* YES. *Hitboxes* has a new choice, **Aimed at** (the unit under the crosshair only), the new default. *Faithful X-COM targeting* is now shown the other way round as **FPS-style shooting (easier)** (NO), so it's clear it changes combat. *Full akimbo* and *Realistic akimbo* are one setting, **Akimbo** (Off, Full, One-hand; the one that was on carries over), and Y does nothing while it's Off. The settings list is in sections: View and camera, Movement, Aiming and combat, Graphics and light, HUD, Sound, Cheats. A player's own saved settings stay as they are.
- **Bullet holes grow with the zoom** (a player, GitHub issue #41: "Zooming in on bullet holes has them APPEAR to become smaller", a heavy laser's especially): a mark up close is drawn no more than 6 pixels from its middle, so it doesn't fill the view, and that cap counted the zoomed view's pixels: zoomed in 4x, the hole stayed as small on the screen while all round it grew. The cap now counts as without the zoom.
- **A clean view** (a player, GitHub issue #37: hiding the HUD left the crosshair and the shot info, "full hudless mode would be useful for a project"): P goes round three steps now: the HUD hidden (the crosshair stays, as before), then a clean view (the crosshair with its lines, the accuracy line and the name under it gone too), then all back.
- **The motion scanner held up in 3D** (GitHub issue #39; the owner: "orient the screen to where player is looking in 3d and make some retro animation like concentric circles from the center and bumping into signals that refresh. And some cool scary beep upon detection (like alien movie)", and "the 3d scanner should not overtake whole screen"): used from the 3D view, the scanner's round screen shows at the bottom of the view, the game going on round it, turning as you look round; a ring sweeps out every two seconds with a whoop, and each movement lights up as the ring reaches it with a pip, higher the nearer. It stays up while it's the item in hand. On the isometric map the game's own, as before.
- **Full sprite hitboxes** (the owner's, GitHub issue #42: "Setting for hit-boxes the same size as sprites!"): a setting (off at first): units are hit, and seen, wherever their full sprites show, a card facing whoever fires, for everyone's shots and line of fire checks, in 3D and on the map; big units too (a Cyberdisc is its disc, not its four tiles).
- **Quick deaths** (a player's report, GitHub issue #40: deaths that don't hold up an auto fire, all at once, "like doomguy chaingunning down zombies"): a setting (off at first): a unit killed or knocked out is down at once, its fall playing on its own at the game's pace with no turn, several at once, in 3D and on the map; the soldier you look through falls on their camera card while the view goes on to the next soldier at once.
- Fixed: the X-COM tape along the map's border read backwards ("MOC-X") on its south and west sides (a player's report, GitHub issue #44); it reads left to right from inside the map on every side.
- **The snap shot back in FPS style** (a player, GitHub issue #28: "Snap shot appears to vanish in favor of hold auto"): with *Faithful X-COM targeting* off, the quick slot (Z) of a gun with an auto shot fired one burst instead of the snap, which a click on AUTO already does; the gun's single snap shot couldn't be fired. Now Z fires the game's snap shot (its time units, the hip's wider spread), as on the map; a click on AUTO fires one burst, held it keeps firing.
- **Melee at floors, ceilings and straight up** (a player, GitHub issue #33: melee "doesn't apply to floors sections above or bellow, or units directly above you on lifts"): the setting **Melee hits floors** (YES, with terrain melee on) lets a blow strike the floor or ceiling under the crosshair, by the game's own hit (the game's terrain melee knows no floors). A unit straight above or below you under the crosshair (flying just above, in a shaft) is struck when the game's own line from the blow reaches it; never through a wall or floor: on a UFO lift the upper pad lies between, so a unit on the next level stays out of reach.
- **A logo of its own** (the owner's): the alien in the yellow X with 3D in its corner, in `docs/logo/` (as supplied, cleaned, and the icon in every size), now the icon of the game's window, the Windows game, the Mac app, the AppImage and the menu entry `install.py --desktop` makes (it replaces OXCE's icon files, so a build from source has it too), and on the web pages.
- **Melee on big units with full sprite hitboxes** (GitHub issue #57: "Melee is impossible, as sprite is considered too far"): melee reach is the game's own, by its own hit shapes, whatever the setting (a 2x2 unit's picture stands in its middle, beyond the tile the reach is measured to). And with the setting on, the game's line of fire checks (the AI choosing a shot, reaction fire, the shot's aim) aim at a big unit's middle, where its picture stands, so it can be shot from every side.
- **Quick deaths' falls at the game's pace** (GitHub issue #58: "should still play death animation at normal speed"): the fall plays at the game's own speed again; nothing waits for it, so a burst still fires on.
- **Akimbo never pairs a gun that blocks both hands** (GitHub issue #48: two pump shotguns were drawn as a pair, then wouldn't fire): a gun the game marks with a red 2 (it won't fire one with the other hand full) fires alone, in 3D and on the map; and *Realistic* is now called **One-hand**, which says what it does.
- **Auto-equip guns first** (GitHub issue #46: soldiers still spawned with claws, shovels and swords in XPiratez and X-Com Files): that's OXCE's own auto-equip, which counts a two-handed melee weapon as a rifle (ours has been OXCE's, unchanged, since 1.05); a new setting, off at first, hands out guns before melee weapons, in the mod's item order (1.04's way).
- **The aliens' turn cuts to a soldier shot at by an unseen alien** (GitHub issue #49: at night "gunfire from unseen units doesn't switch to units who can see the gun fire"): with *Aliens' turn cameras*, an alien none of your soldiers sees firing at one of them cuts the view to that soldier at once, turned to where the fire comes from (it only popped up their camera card).
- **HIDDEN MOVEMENT glows green on the civilians' turn** (GitHub issue #55): as the map's turn bar turns green for the civilians, the banner's glow does too; red and purple on the aliens' turn as before.
- **Crops and rubble without a shape stand as the map draws them** (GitHub issues #56 and #52: X-Com Files' and XPiratez's crop fields, broken glass, showed as nothing; the owner: the isometric map is the reference): with *Parts without shape* at Lifts (the default) or All, a low part the game gives no voxels, no higher than a knee (crops, rubble heaps, the debris of a blown wall, a scatter of glass), stands in 3D: each column of its art where it meets the floor, as tall as the art there, drawn solid as on the map, not dotted like a lift pad; shots, sight and walking still go through it, as in the game. Art lying flat on the floor (paint, shards of glass) is laid on top of the floor.
- **Wheat fields as a block** (GitHub issue #56: X-Com Files' and XPiratez's farms "have no wheat"; checked in X-Com Files 4.2 itself): their wheat is UFO's, its art a block of wheat as wide as the tile and about 14 voxels tall, the game giving it a floor's slab and four thin posts, so 3D showed sparse posts. With *Parts without shape* at Lifts or All, a walkable object drawn as a tile-wide block 4 to 16 voxels tall whose own voxels fill under half of it stands as a solid block (the owner's choice): the field's golden top at the wheat's height, its ears standing on it, sides where the field ends; standing you look over it, whoever is in it shows from the waist up, kneeling you see wheat.
